#2998. Top Wall Street analysts suggest these 3 dividend stocks for steady income

The ongoing earnings season, investor concerns about the durability of AI demand and spending, and geopolitical risks are key factors that have been contributing to stock market volatility in recent trading sessions.

In this scenario, investors seeking steady income can consider adding dividend stocks to their portfolios. Recommendations of top Wall Street analysts can help them pick attractive dividend stocks that are backed by solid cash flows to support consistent payments.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Oil and gas exploration and production company ConocoPhillips is this week's first dividend pick. With a dividend of 84 cents per share (annualized dividend of $3.36 per share), COP offers a dividend yield of 3%. The company is scheduled to announce its second-quarter results on Aug. 6.

Ahead of second-quarter results, Wells Fargo analyst Sam Margolin reiterated a buy rating on COP stock with a price target of $183. Despite the pressure on oil prices from an increase in OPEC production quota, the analyst finds ConocoPhillips and Shell stocks appealing as the earnings season approaches. He cited their operational visibility and resilience as factors backing their appeal.

The 5-star analyst expects ConocoPhillips to meet its production guidance of 2.2 million barrels of oil equivalent per day at the mid-point. He expects lower Waha natural gas prices in the Permian Basin to be offset by stronger Brent crude premiums. Margolin expects capital expenditure to remain within COP's prior guided range of $12.2 billion annualized, with no significant impact on spending on the Northfield East project in Qatar despite the Strait of Hormuz disruption.

Overall, Margolin expects COP to generate about $3.5 billion in free cash flow (before working capital) and earnings per share of $2.94. He expects continued strength in COP's free cash flow and regular dividend growth through the completion of the Willow project in 2028/2029. Prior to the Willow project coming online, the analyst expects free cash flow to grow by about $2 billion in 2027 and 2028, assuming Brent crude averages around $60 per barrel.

"COP's track record of capital efficiency and strong Permian well productivity underpins its ability to pursue long-cycle developments," said Margolin.

Margolin ranks No. 457 among more than 12,300 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 70% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.3%. See ConocoPhillips Financials on TipRanks.

Energy Transfer is a limited partnership that operates 140,000 miles of pipeline and associated energy infrastructure. With a quarterly cash distribution of 33.75 cents per common unit ($1.35 per unit on an annualized basis), ET offers a yield of 6.8%.

Heading into Energy Transfer's Q2 earnings on Aug. 4, Jefferies analyst Julien Dumoulin-Smith reaffirmed a buy rating on ET stock with a price target of $23. The analyst noted that his adjusted earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization estimate of $4.46 billion is 1% below the Street's consensus of $4.49 billion.

The 5-star analyst noted that Energy Transfer has slightly outperformed Enterprise Products Partners recently. However, it still trades at a relative discount of 19% compared to EPD, which is below its historical discount range of 17%-20%. Smith believes that ET stock could be re-rated higher if the company provides a clearer long-term strategy for natural gas growth.

Furthermore, Smith expects the current energy market to support a stronger outlook for natural gas liquids and crude oil. "The current energy macro backdrop positions ET to benefit in all three commodities," said the analyst.

He expects Energy Transfer's adjusted EBITDA to grow at a 4.8% compound annual growth rate in 2027-2030, which is 1%-3% above Wall Street's expectations. In fact, Smith sees the possibility of additional upside if ET announces more natural gas projects. He added that investors will await details on final investment decisions on new natural gas projects and any clues about additional projects in the pipeline. The analyst noted that ET has announced new gas projects consistently in recent quarters.

Smith ranks No. 550 among more than 12,300 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.4%. See Energy Transfer Statistics on TipRanks.

Finally, let's look at energy giant Chevron, which is scheduled to announce its second-quarter results on July 31. Last month, the company paid a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share. At an annualized dividend of $7.12, CVX offers a dividend yield of 3.92%.

Ahead of Q2 earnings, Jefferies analyst Lloyd Byrne reiterated a buy rating on Chevron stock and lowered his price target to $216 from $236. Byrne expects the company to report adjusted EPS of about $5.86 per share, nearly 9% above the Street's expectations.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that the challenges seen in Chevron's upstream business in the first quarter due to the disruption at the Tengizchevroil joint venture in Kazakhstan, Storm Fern downtime, and the Middle East conflict have largely been resolved. Consequently, Byrne expects production to recover in the second quarter to about 4,033 mboepd. He expects the upstream business to generate adjusted earnings of about $8.1 billion in Q2 2026.

Meanwhile, Byrne expects Chevron to generate downstream adjusted earnings of about $4.4 billion in Q2, with strength in both domestic and international markets. The downstream business benefited from higher crack spreads and strong refining performance.

Additionally, the analyst expects Chevron to generate $18.2 billion in cash flow from operations (before working capital changes), driven by stronger earnings and about $2.2 billion in dividends from affiliated companies. Unlike the first quarter, Chevron is not expected to make a TCO loan repayment in Q2, providing an additional boost to cash flow.

Byrne ranks No. 409 among more than 12,300 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 56% of the time, delivering an average return of 17.5%. See Chevron Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

#2997. Federal Reserve Board requests public comment on two proposals related to establishing a regulatory framework for Board-supervised payment stablecoin issuers under the GENIUS Act

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 2:30 p.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday requested public comment on two proposals related to establishing a regulatory framework for Board-supervised payment stablecoin issuers under the GENIUS Act.

The first proposal would require that Board-supervised payment stablecoin issuers fully back their stablecoins with certain permissible reserve assets, such as short-term Treasury bills and certain other high-quality, liquid assets. The proposal would also establish standardized capital requirements to address certain credit and operational risks of payment stablecoin activities, as well as risk management standards, in accordance with the law. Separately, the proposal would introduce rules for Board-supervised firms that safekeep the assets backing payment stablecoins. It would also clarify the permissibility of stablecoin and related activities for Board-supervised banks.

The second proposal would establish a tailored application process for Board-supervised banks applying to issue payment stablecoins. These applicants would be required to submit a business plan and financial information, among other documents. The proposal would also create a process governing appeals, hearings, and final determinations for applications.

The comment period will close 60 days after publication in the Federal Register.

For media inquiries, please email [email protected] or call (202) 452-2955.

#2995. Luka Dončić gave an encouraging health update after injury scare vs. Bucks

Late in the fourth quarter of the Los Angeles Lakers' 123-112 win against the Milwaukee Bucks Wednesday night, Luka Dončić went to the locker room with an ankle injury after Kyle Kuzma ran him over on a pretty careless closeout.

Despite not returning once he took and hit his free throw, Dončić made clear there's nothing to worry about moving forward.

"I'm good," he told reporters postgame "Obviously, it's painful a little bit, but I'll be fine."

Off to a roaring first three weeks, Dončić is powering the Lakers' West-leading 11-2 start and current seven-game winning streak. With Dončić on the floor this season, Los Angeles has outscored its opponents by 165 points in 395 minutes. When he sits, it's been outscored by 62 points in 229 minutes.

If you'd like the small-sample theatre for those numbers, that's a plus-19.3 net rating with him and a minus-11.1 net rating without him. The Lakers are posting a ridiculous 131.0 offensive rating during his minutes. Yet they've also been much better defensively in that span, allowing 111.6 points per 100 possessions with him and 120.0 per 100 without him.

That's largely the product of Dončić playing career-best defense as he's drawing charges, inhaling rebounds and consistently occupying the passing lanes or rotating punctually off the ball to generate turnovers. Only Brandin Podziemski (five) has drawn more charges than Dončić (four) while his 2.4% steal rate and 1.1% block rate are career highs, per Cleaning the Glass.

Although the on-off numbers are staggering and the Lakers will certainly be glad Dončić has avoided injury, they have survived the two games he's sat out thus far. Austin Reaves scored 43 points Sunday to lead them past a very good Detroit Pistons squad and they trounced the Los Angeles Clippers, 103-87, back on Oct. 28.

That's not to downplay Dončić's importance but rather to highlight the Lakers' depth and their adaptability. Dončić is a supernova but 11-2, including seven straight wins, doesn't happen with a one-man show. Los Angeles is a well-rounded group and has received key contributions almost every night from its supporting cast. It'll look to keep that going when its road trip comes to a close Sunday against the Phoenix Suns at 7 p.m.

You can follow Jackson on Twitter at @jackfrank_jjf.

#2994. Oil prices rise after Trump says no Iran deal needed: 'We've got their Strait'

U.S. oil prices rose Thursday, as comments from President Donald Trump stoked worries that an end to the U.S.-Iran war was not in sight.

Brent crude traded 1.9% higher at $94.48 per barrel. West Texas Intermediate futures were up 2% at $88.19 per barrel.

"We don't need a deal with Iran. Those days have long since passed. We've got their money, Hundreds of Billions of Dollars, and we've got their Strait!" Trump said in a Truth Social post.

Jeffrey Currie, founder of Real Macro and a widely followed forecaster on Wall Street, said oil prices in the near term will remain tame because there is now a substantial amount of volume coming out of the Middle East. However, he noted that sky-high insurance and tanker rates are keeping prices elevated on longer-dated oil futures contracts.

"While it caps the prompt barrel, it does nothing to cheapen the next one, which is why you can't get bearish here nor can you get really bullish," said Currie to CNBC's "The Exchange."

On the investing front, Currie said he still favors long-dated oil and the drilling and producer stocks.

Thursday's moves come as oil prices have been under pressure after comments from Trump following the U.S. midterm elections eased fears of further strikes on Iran. WTI is down 16% in November, and Brent has lost 20%.

Trump said Sunday that fresh attacks were highly unlikely as talks to end the conflict progressed, despite both ⁠countries reporting that shipping had been hit in or near the Strait of Hormuz. He also said further military action did not appear necessary "at this point," easing concerns about prolonged disruptions to Middle East oil supplies.

John Kilduff, a partner at Again Capital, said investors may have been too quick to price in an end to the fighting.

"The market may have gotten ahead of itself on pricing in a cessation of hostilities," Kilduff told CNBC. He warned that renewed doubts about a deal would push the security premium in oil prices higher.

Trump's assertion that the U.S. controls the Strait of Hormuz could spur further Iranian attacks on shipping, increasing the risk of prolonged supply disruptions, according to Kilduff.

"The President may be thinking that given enough time, the economic blockade of Iran will force them to either negotiate with the USA or result in a regime change as the Iranian economy collapses. The Iranians might have different ideas and continue to attack Middle East Infrastructure that results in higher prices and forces the President to the negotiating table," said Andy Lipow, president of Lipow Oil Associates.

"In any event, it does not seem to me that either side's negotiating position has changed. Iran wants the blockade to end, sanctions to be lifted and a ceasefire including Lebanon to be declared. The USA wants Iran to get rid of its entire nuclear stockpile and allow free transit through the Strait of Hormuz," he said.

—CNBC's Pippa Stevens contributed to the report.

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation and the Federal Reserve Board today published feedback letters for resolution plans submitted in October 2025. The agencies did not identify any shortcomings or deficiencies in these resolution plan submissions.

Resolution plans, also known as living wills, describe a banking organization's strategy for orderly resolution in the event of material financial distress or failure. The agencies conducted a joint review of the 2025 resolution plan submissions from 15 banking organizations with over $250 billion in assets.

#2989. Federal Reserve Board finalizes changes to enhance the transparency and public accountability of its stress test and reduce volatility in its stress test-related capital requirements

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 9:00 a.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Wednesday finalized changes to enhance the transparency and public accountability of its stress test and reduce volatility in its stress test-related capital requirements. The two final rules are largely similar to the proposed rules from 2025. The Board also requested comment on a proposal to better capture differences in banks' business models to generate fee income.

The Board conducts stress tests to ensure that large banks are sufficiently capitalized and able to lend to households and businesses even in a severe recession. In December 2024, the Board announced it would modify the test to improve its resilience.

"The stress test is an essential component of our regulatory capital framework," said Vice Chair for Supervision Michelle W. Bowman. "Today's changes preserve its resilience by ensuring that it is transparent, granular, and risk-sensitive. The public will now have greater assurance that the risks banks take will be reflected appropriately in their stress test losses and their capital requirements."

The first final rule requires the Board to invite public input annually on the stress test scenarios and any material model changes. It also updates the framework that guides the design of the hypothetical scenarios and adopts the models that will be used for the 2027 stress test. In addition, the final rule makes several adjustments to the stress test calendar. The final rule also updates the global market shock component, which is a set of shocks to specific market components applied to banks with large trading books. These banks will now be tested against two global market shock components each year, and the Board will use the shock that produces the largest losses for each firm to calculate the firm's stress test results.

The second final rule requires the Board, when calculating stress capital buffer requirements, to average the results from the two most recent annual supervisory stress tests for firms subject to the stress test in both years. The Board will begin averaging stress capital buffer requirements in 2028 to ensure that only models incorporating public input are used in the calculation.

The proposal would request public comment on a revision to the Board's noninterest income model that would improve the stress test's ability to capture business model diversity across firms. If adopted, these adjustments would replace the current noninterest income model that projects each bank's fee income under stress. Comments are due 60 days after publication in the Federal Register.

Together, the changes are likely to reduce year-over-year volatility in capital requirements by approximately 50 percent and are not expected to materially affect aggregate capital requirements.

For media inquiries, please email [email protected] or call 202-452-2955.

#2987. Bain Capital’s head of real estate uses this strategy to counter the economy’s ‘cocktail of disruption'

A version of this article first appeared in the CNBC Property Play newsletter with Diana Olick. Property Play covers new and evolving opportunities for the real estate investor, from individuals to venture capitalists, private equity funds, family offices, institutional investors and large public companies. Sign up to receive future editions, straight to your inbox.

Over the last few years, macro shocks have rocked the U.S. economy about every six months, from tariffs to the Iran war to the Democratic sweep of the midterm elections. All of that has a direct impact on real estate, according to Ryan Cotton, Bain Capital's head of real estate.

Cotton oversees $18 billion of real estate assets under management. In this week's Property Play video podcast, Cotton says that every time the market starts to get back on its feet and find its balance and level, a new shock comes around, and there's another digestion period that has to happen.

"I think it is disorienting as people try to rebase values, and in the midst of transactions, you're seeing value retrades at the 11th hour, deals getting pulled, and volume slowing down yet again in the market," said Cotton. "It's definitely a confusing and complicated time to be a real estate investor."

The overall market is still working through an oversupply in several sectors, which was brought on by extremely low interest rates at the start of this decade. There are also shifting demand patterns, with artificial intelligence changing the way logistics, supply chains, and offices work, and where wealth is being created and spent in the economy.

"All those things put together are creating this cocktail of disruption in real estate that's made things disruptive for the industry as a whole, I think both in terms of understanding the value of what people own today in this new paradigm but also thinking about where do you go from here, and how do you allocate capital against those dynamic shifts?" Cotton said.

So how does Cotton navigate all that? He said Bain is a long-term investor, not a spot trader of assets. He looks at real estate over a ten-year period, evaluating the secular demand drivers that create the need for specific types of real estate.

"And if you take that lens, it leads you away from the office, it leads you away from the mall, it leads you candidly away from some multifamily markets where you have out-migration or oversupply of that product, and instead it leads you into more of what I would call alternative or emerging classes of real estate," said Cotton.

Senior housing, self-storage and student housing are some of the more traditional but alternative classes he likes. But what really excites him are the super alternatives: Private airplane hangars and marinas for boats and yachts.

"Wealth is concentrating, and as people get richer, they buy more toys," he explained. "It can generate double-digit NOI [net operating income] growth in those assets, and there is real scarcity of those assets and supply barriers. That means that even though demand continues to grow, supply may not have an adequate response to it."

He points to recent deals involving Atlantic Aviation, the second-largest private airplane hangar platform in the U.S., and Safe Harbor, the largest marina platform in the country. The latter was bought by Blackstone this year.

Ten years ago, Cotton mused, something like 93% of all real estate assets in the world were invested in the four major food groups, being multifamily, office, retail and industrial. Now that's down to 82%.

"More and more capital is moving out of those sectors, because of the lack of structural demand drivers, and moving into these more peripheral asset classes," he said. "The 10 largest [REITs] in the world, seven of them are alternative sectors of real estate, be that data centers, be that senior housing. These are not traditional four food group asset classes, and that's where the most liquid markets have migrated."

Cotton leans on the idea of "Mr. Market," a thought experiment invented by financial analyst Benjamin Graham and popularized by Warren Buffett to illustrate the swings of the stock market by imagining it as a person. "When he's a bully, you can sell, and when he's depressed you can just tell him to get out of your office," said Cotton. "If you put your head down and work hard on creating value, I'm certain that eventually the market finds its level, finds its balance, and Mr. Market finds his optimism again, and then you can get back into transacting."

CNBC's Property Play with Diana Olick covers new and evolving opportunities for the real estate investor, delivered weekly to your inbox.

#2985. Mike Brown is sticking with Mikal Bridges. For now.

In last night's loss to the Cavaliers, Mikal Bridges played 17 minutes, took three shots, and finished with two points, two rebounds, and an assist. This was another disappointing performance in a season when we expected the wing to make his first All-Star appearance.

At his presser after the game, coach Mike Brown said: "Mikal is a starter, and so it's not a question of that. He's like a lot of players. They've had their good moments and their not-so-good moments. And Mikal has a proven track record of playing at a pretty high level as a starter in this league. So, for me to make a quote-unquote panic decision and change it right now, we're ten games in, or whatever we are, I'm not feeling it."

And in the locker room, Jalen Brunson defended his teammate. "The people in this room have his back night in & night out. We see the work he puts into his game, […] Regardless of what the noise is on the outside, that's our brother."

Bridges is an iron man. Between college ball and the pros, he hasn't missed a game. He's also one of Jalen Brunson's closest friends on the team, and as a Villanova teammates they won two national titles together. Kal's mantle is full of pre-pro awards, and he was named to the NBA All-Defensive First Team in 2022. Bridges is a world champion. With a lithe, long body, he's a tremendous cutter, plays a terrific midrange game, and guards multiple positions well. He's a career 48% / 37% / 84% shooter, averaging over 14 points and 1.2 steals per game. Maybe not Hall-of-Fame, but definitely Hall-of-Pretty-Good.

In 2024, Brooklyn traded him to New York for a boatload of picks. In just over two seasons at his current home, he's averaged more than 15 points per game and has been a fixture of the starting lineup.

He's also been consistently inconsistent. There were times last season when we thought it might be time to sacrifice the games-played streak for a little RnR. After the first five games of the playoffs, we were ready to leave him behind in Atlanta. Then he went on an incredible run, and aside from faltering somewhat against the Spurs, he was essential to New York bringing home a championship. As we know, winning heals a lot of wounds. Those same fans who complained about the overpay soon took up a delirious chant about defiling those picks.

You cannot deny the man's achievements—nor that he's stunk so far this season. After a summer of celebration, the 30-year-old has started the current campaign with some of the worst play of his career. Eleven games in, his stats are 5.9 PPG, 33% FG, and 27% 3PT. These Knicks are notoriously late starters, often not hitting their stride until late in the first or even second quarter. Or the fourth. Cap even joked about this in his SNL appearance. But this time, Mikal has no stride to hit. Lately, he has watched the fourth quarter action from the bench (very expensive seats, indeed).

Mike Brown is a supreme diplomat, saying all the right things at all times. Yet, Mikal is a starter because the coach chooses to start him. That's the coach's prerogative. The skipper could field someone else as the starting shooting guard—say Landry Shamet, who's proven himself more than worthy. Why hasn't he?

First, there's a chemistry to the team that Mike Brown handles like plutonium. To disrupt the starting five by separating Kal from his 'Nova brethren might yield positive results on the court, but could foment discord behind the scenes. A demotion could also further erode Bridges' confidence, which would be bad for their long-term plans. Unless he's shipped off before the midseason trade deadline (more musings on that below), the Knicks will need Bridges in shape physically and mentally for a deep postseason run.

Further, there's the potential loss of reliable sparkplug off the bench. For a while now, Shamet's been the most consistent in that role. McBride and Clarkson have certainly had their moments, too, but when Mike needs a sub AND a bucket AND defense, ShamWow has become his security blanket. When the score is close, Landry's steady contribution makes it difficult to justify giving those two-guard minutes to Bridges in the final frame.

Here's Brown again, from his postgame. "Yeah, part of it is we got two guys that are playing really well in Landry and Deuce, and so it's hard. [… They] have been a big part of what we've done defensively. If they're playing at a high level, I got to keep trying to find ways to get those guys on the floor."

As Brown alluded, the Knicks have more guards than they need. Conversely, they remain light at the center position. They have Karl-Anthony Towns playing a career year, Andre Drummond backing him up with great rebounds and questionable shots, and . . . James Wiseman? Sometimes? Leon Rose & Co. are aware of this. They also know that in a trade for a big man of talent, they will need both a desirable player to offer and one whose salary helps to offset what's incoming.

Obviously, Bridges is a nice guy. He's not so diabolical as to throw his season to insulate himself from being traded. His sub-standard play does complicate trade opportunities, however. Another club that's willing to part with a talented center will want to take on a positive, productive player, not a distressed asset with a lot of basketball miles on his wheels.

There's also a not insignificant chance that the front office is totally cool with what they have and are unwilling to make a panic move either. After all, the team that won the Finals did so with Karl-Anthony Towns, occasional Mitchell Robinson, and a sprinkling of Ariel Hukporti. If Brown can coach Wiseman up to Huk-level participation, then what's the difference, really?

When asked about his play earlier in this short season, Bridges said, "Obviously we should be better than we are, me personally," he said. "But it takes time and just not skipping steps."

He doesn't sound panicked. Neither does Mike after 11 games. Nor Jalen, Leon, or the rest of the team. What about 15 games? 20? Can we run screaming down the street on December 1st? Ideally, Bridges gets right in Friday's game against Indy, kills the conversation for the rest of the season, and we all look back and laugh. If this continues to be an issue, though, we're very interested to see Mike's next move.

#2983. Top Wall Street analysts back these 3 stocks for their long-term growth potential

Major indexes remain volatile as investors assess key earnings releases and geopolitical tensions in the Middle East. Investors with a long-term horizon should look beyond the ongoing noise and focus on stocks that are well-positioned to generate strong returns.

In this regard, recommendations of top Wall Street analysts can help shortlist stocks with robust growth opportunities. These experts conduct an in-depth analysis of all the macro and micro factors affecting a company, focusing on its long-term growth potential.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

CrowdStrike Holdings is a cloud-native cybersecurity provider. The company recently announced an expanded partnership with Schwarz Digits to bring its Falcon platform to European enterprises.

After hosting several European investor meetings with the CrowdStrike CFO, Stifel analyst Adam Borg reiterated a buy rating on CRWD stock and increased his price target to $230 from $220.

The five-star analyst said that the meetings increased his confidence about CrowdStrike being an "important, well-positioned cybersecurity platform vendor that is an AI beneficiary given its expansive portfolio, unique data set, and ability to both secure AI and use AI to improve security."

Among the key takeaways, Borg noted that AI is driving a major shift in cybersecurity demand as it presents software vulnerabilities that can be exploited by less sophisticated hackers with capabilities that were once limited to government-backed hacking groups. This has led to companies making cybersecurity a top priority, driving customer interest in CrowdStrike's new AI Detection & Response solution and its broader security platform.

Borg added that AI-driven demand is expanding CrowdStrike's pipeline and supporting confidence in the company's higher fiscal 2027 net new annual recurring revenue guidance issued last quarter. Overall, Borg believes that CrowdStrike has multiple growth drivers that can help maintain revenue growth in at least the high-teens and drive improved profitability in the years ahead.

Borg ranks No. 651 among more than 12,300 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 65% of the time, delivering an average return of 15%. See CrowdStrike Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Moving on to AST SpaceMobile, a company building a global cellular broadband network in space. Recently, Piper Sandler analyst Alexander Potter initiated coverage of space-related stocks. While the analyst assigned AST SpaceMobile a buy rating with a price target of $100, he has a neutral stance on rocket builders SpaceX and Rocket Lab due to valuation concerns.

"We prefer ASTS, due to a more palatable valuation, and a clearer path to EBITDA upside," said Potter.

The five-star analyst highlighted that AST SpaceMobile builds satellites that connect directly to smartphones, ensuring network connectivity for users everywhere and enabling applications like video calls, streaming, and gaming. Potter added that ASTS' technology helps Mobile Network Operators, or MNOs, expand coverage and offer add-on services to travelers and users in remote locations.

Potter ranks No. 708 among more than 12,300 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 48% of the time, delivering an average return of 17.2%. See AST SpaceMobile Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

Finally, let's look at Broadcom, a provider of custom AI chips and networking solutions. Recently, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reiterated a buy rating on Broadcom stock with a price target of $502.

The five-star analyst is surprised by the underperformance of AVGO stock so far this year compared to its peers, especially given continued momentum in AI-driven demand. Moore cited concerns that competitor MediaTek could gain market share from Broadcom in Google's tensor processing unit chip business and investor preference for faster-growing AI chip names as reasons for the underperformance.

While Moore acknowledged that MediaTek's role in Google's TPU business is growing, he contends that it is unlikely to disrupt Broadcom's position significantly. He expects Broadcom to continue to be the major TPU supplier, with about 80% market share. Overall, Moore believes that concerns about a decline in AVGO's market share to 50% or its eventual replacement seem premature.

"AVGO should retain majority share, sustain strong AI growth, and remain a core compute winner, close behind NVDA," said Moore.

The analyst explained that his bullish outlook on Broadcom is based on the company's solid advantage in high-bandwidth memory supply, chip packaging capabilities, and large-scale production. Moore also highlighted AVGO's dominance in AI ASIC chips, strong networking business, and new customer wins.

Moore ranks No. 148 among more than 12,300 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 60% of the time, delivering an average return of 25.5%. See Broadcom Insider Trading Activity on TipRanks.

#2979. This move has aided the development of Khaman Maluach and the Phoenix Suns

When the Phoenix Suns kicked off the season, the front court had already seen some setbacks. Last year's starting center, Mark Williams, who earned a nice extension, was reportedly out with a torn labrum. This would force the Suns to sign Duop Reath to a two-way contract to ensure they had a third center on the roster for opening night. Not only that, but it raised an even bigger question: who would start on opening night? Would it be Oso Ighodaro, the veteran big man who took over as the starter late in the season, or Khaman Maluach, the lottery selection from the Suns in the 2025 draft, who is set to be the center of the future?

That question was answered when Khaman Maluach saw the floor with the starters as the Suns took on the Trail Blazers on opening night. Unfortunately for the Suns and Maluach, the lights were brighter than expected, and the team was run off the floor basically in the first quarter. The Suns bounced back in their next game against Golden State, but with Ighodaro injured in the opener, the center rotation was thin. With the Suns also facing the Oklahoma City Thunder and Denver Nuggets, this didn't help, as Maluach was forced to play against top-tier bigs and defensive teams, which disrupted his chance to build momentum.

When Ighodaro returned from injury, many fans expected him to get the starting job, but Maluach still started. This continued for a few more games until game seven, when the Suns faced the Orlando Magic. This was a must-win for the Suns, as they had just lost at home the night before to the Portland Trail Blazers in embarrassing fashion.

In this game, the Suns found their offensive groove and punished a team that couldn't make a shot defensively, taking command and winning to show fans they are the team they have been preaching about. A big reason was plugging in Oso Ighodaro as the starting center; his screening allowed Devin Booker to hunt his shots early and score 23 first-half points. What was nice to see, though, was that Maluach looked more comfortable with the bench unit.

Against the Magic, he had eight points (4-for-6 FG), six rebounds, two assists, and two steals, and finished with a +13 in 22 minutes, which is solid for him as he settles into that new role. His active hands helped on defense, and even though he struggled against Wendell Carter Jr. to get all the boards, he put up some fight, which was nice to see. In his next opportunity against the Rockets, though, he showed that this potential can be great with this bench mob.

Versus the Rockets, he had eight points (3-for-4 FG), ten rebounds, one steal, two blocks, and finished with a +11 in 18 minutes. Once again, he seemed more comfortable with the bench unit and hungry to grab boards. Without Steven Adams and Clint Capela, the Suns still struggled against a tall Rockets team, but Malauch held his own and kept fighting for opportunities. He also did a solid job on the defensive end against Alperen Sengun, really limiting him until the final quarter. His active hands helped him get three stocks, and that trend will continue to help his stock rise in fans' eyes.

After two solid games, the Suns took on last year's Western Conference champion, the San Antonio Spurs, and it was a rough one for them ultimately. However, Maluach looked solid (as solid as a sophomore backup big can be) in minutes against Victor Wembanyama and gave Suns fans something to look forward to. Even if he only had five rebounds, he scored 11 points (4-for-6 FG) and showed his range by hitting his only three-point attempt.

The game that really made this clear, though, was the latest one against the Los Angeles Clippers. Similar to the Blazers game they lost, the Suns needed to win the second half of the back-to-back in commanding fashion, just like they did against the Magic. Luckily, the Suns did, beating them 133-110, and once again Man Man showed his ability to be impactful. In just 22 minutes, he posted 11 points (4-5, 0-for-1 3FG) and went 100% from the FT line in back-to-back nights. Add seven boards and three assists to that night, and you see his nightly growth.

He has to show he can beat down these bad teams, and he went up against Isaiah Jackson for most of the night. In this recent game, he showed his knack for keeping the fight on the glass and the awareness to recognize when to make a smart pass. Not only that, but his two-person game with Collin Gillepsie has really grown, with CG finding easy passes for two points in the paint for Man Man. Truly, this switch has not only helped Maluach and his development to find himself and not be rushed to go against the Nikola Jokics of the world yet, but also aids the starters as well.

With Ighodaro now thrust into the starting lineup, the Suns have someone who isn't going to play hero ball. They have someone who will do the dirty work and make the smart plays with them, which is what they need. Ighodaro's screening has received high praise from Booker this season, and it gives him the freedom to get to his spots efficiently and wide open. His passing also helps, as he can set up valuable back-door cuts, find the right man, or make the extra pass. This allows the offense to move the ball more, stay more fluid as a unit, and generate looks for the open man.

All in all, this move has been beneficial, as a healthy lineup of Gillespie, Goodwin, Kennard, Dunn, and Maluach can rival some of the league's best benches. This will benefit Maluach as he learns to grow and become a starter in the future. Gillespie's ability to set up Ighodaro and Williams in the past, when they were on the bench, makes me confident he can do the same with Maluach. You also add that this move makes the starters look a bit better, and it's pure joy. For Man Man, I think this was the best move of his young career, and he'll have fans buy into his potential as a true centerpiece of this team, and I cannot wait to see that unfold before our eyes.

#2976. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 dividend stocks for passive income

The uncertainty in the Middle East and concerns about the durability of the artificial intelligence boom and high spending continue to weigh on investor sentiment. Investors can consider strengthening their portfolios by adding dividend stocks.

Tracking top Wall Street analysts can help investors shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as these experts provide useful insights after thorough analysis of a company's financials and growth prospects.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first dividend pick is natural gas producer Expand Energy, which recently announced the $1.25 billion acquisition of Twin Eagle Holdings, a private asset-backed natural gas marketing and optimization business. The deal is expected to accelerate EXE's marketing and commercial goals.

Meanwhile, Expand Energy impressed investors with solid second-quarter results. It repurchased shares worth $530 million in Q2 2026 and announced an additional $1 billion of buyback authorization. Expand declared a dividend of nearly 58 cents per share, payable on Sept. 3. At an annualized dividend per share of $2.30, EXE stock offers a dividend yield of 2.5%.

Following the Q2 print, Wolfe Research analyst Doug Leggate reiterated a buy rating on EXE stock and raised his price target to $114 from $110. The five-star analyst noted that Expand delivered market-beating adjusted earnings per share and earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation and amortization, driven by better gas realizations and lower gathering, processing, and transportation expenses.

"Solid 2Q26 with in-line guidance keeps our focus on what it can control, walking the line between options to return value to s/holders," said Leggate.

As Expand Energy continues to weigh options to enhance shareholder returns, Leggate believes that net debt reduction is the best choice. He highlighted that the company repaid $1.3 billion of gross debt in April, supported by seasonally higher cash flows in the first quarter. EXE ended Q2 2026 with $3.1 billion in net debt. Leggate views buybacks as the second-best option and mergers and acquisitions as the third, with the Twin Eagle deal expected to lower Expand's breakeven by about 7 cents from about $2.80/Mcf.

Leggate ranks No. 807 among more than 12,400 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.1%. See Expand Energy Stock Buybacks on TipRanks.

Moving on to SM Energy, an operator of oil and gas assets across four leading U.S. shale basins: the Permian Basin, DJ Basin, South Texas, and Uinta Basin. SM offers a quarterly dividend of 22 cents per share, or an annualized dividend of 88 cents per share, representing a yield of about 2.7%.

Following a preliminary update on Q2 metrics, Roth analyst Leo Mariani reaffirmed a buy rating on SM Energy stock and raised his price target to $34 from $32. The analyst cited higher oil prices and his optimism about a strong second-quarter report as the reasons for the upgraded price target.

Commenting on the key insights from the Q2 preliminary update, Mariani noted strong oil and gas price realizations in the quarter. However, the update reflected a slightly larger cash hedging loss of $220 million from derivatives, compared to Roth's estimate of about $211 million.

Meanwhile, the five-star analyst raised his cash flow per share estimate by 3%, citing a strong Q2 2026 pricing update. Mariani added that he expects SM Energy to report strong Q2 2026 results on Aug. 5, with oil production of 237,650 barrels of oil per day, which is around 1.5% above the Street's estimate. He expects capital expenditure of $820 million, in line with consensus.

"We rate SM Energy a Buy based on its reasonable returns of capital to shareholders, its discounted valuation vs. peers, and its upside potential in the Austin Chalk and Uinta plays," said Mariani.

Mariani ranks No. 65 among more than 12,400 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 67% of the time, delivering an average return of 27%. See SM Energy Financials on TipRanks.

Oilfield services provider SLB, formerly known as Schlumberger, reported better-than-expected Q2 earnings. The company attributed its Q2 performance to sequential growth across international markets, mainly led by offshore activity in Latin America, Europe and Africa, as well as Asia, helping offset disruptions in the Middle East due to the U.S.-Iran conflict.

SLB announced a quarterly cash dividend of almost 30 cents per share, payable on Oct. 8. At an annualized dividend per share of $1.18, SLB's yield stands at 2.4%.

Reacting to the Q2 earnings, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reaffirmed a buy rating on SLB stock with a price target of $62. "Given SLB's position within international OFS, we reiterate our Buy rating and see forward activity expectations as a driver of earnings," said the analyst.

Within Goldman's oilfield services coverage, Mehta is optimistic about SLB as he expects the company to benefit from a recovery in international activity. The five-star analyst projects international revenue growth of about 10% between 2026 and 2027, driven by SLB's exposure to incremental intervention activity and a recovery in Middle East activity. He also expects international revenue to gain from higher offshore activity and a potential ramp-up of exploration activity across several geographies.

Furthermore, Mehta's bullish stance is backed by long-term growth prospects of SLB's data center business. He noted that the company expects the annual run-rate revenue of this business to exceed $2 billion by the end of 2027, with the potential to grow the customer base and products. Mehta also expects SLB to deliver strong free cash flow and shareholder returns this year, driven by higher activity and unique business lines.

Mehta ranks No. 666 among more than 12,400 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.4%. See SLB Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

#2975. Nebius falls 16% as company expects increased 2026 capex spend, issues weak outlook

Nebius shares sank 16% on Thursday as one of the biggest publicly traded neoclouds issued disappointing full-year revenue guidance.

Here's how the company performed relative to LSEG consensus:

Revenue jumped 571% in the quarter, according to a statement. The company reported a net loss of $37.5 million, a loss of 13 cents per share, compared with $119.6 million, a loss of 50 cents per share, a year earlier.

The stock move also comes amid the resignation of Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang, after stinging revelations about multiple extramarital affairs, including one with an alleged Chinese spy.

Nvidia, which has developed the neocloud market by investing in the companies and backstopping some of their projects, held about $329 million in Nebius shares at the end of June, according to a filing.

In a letter to shareholders, CEO Arkady Volozh said the company expects fiscal year 2026 revenue at the low end of the company's $3 billion to $3.4 billion range. Analysts surveyed by LSEG had $3.37 billion.

Nebius also shared an initial outlook for 2027, expecting revenue of $12 billion to $13 billion, "reflecting a conservative approach to the pace of our buildout." The LSEG consensus was $11.98 billion.

The company expects FY2026 capital expenditures of $27 billion, higher than its previous capex guidance of $20 billion to $25 billion and the FactSet expectation of $23.85 billion.

Nebius' expected FY2027 capex of $40 billion to $45 billion came in much higher than the FactSet expectation of $28.77 billion.

Nebius' results come after fellow neoclouds CoreWeave and IREN reported better-than-expected financials in their earnings announcements over the past week. That's provided a boost to the companies' stocks, which had suffered steep selloffs in recent weeks on concern about the sustainability of the artificial intelligence buildout.

The neocloud companies posted a robust rally on Wednesday, with Coreweave booming nearly 25%, Nebius climbing 15%, Applied Digital rising more than 7% and IREN adding nearly 6%.

Each of the stocks fell Thursday premarket after the Nebius Q3 report.

Last week, CoreWeave's bond yields spiked past the 16% mark. In its earnings report on Tuesday, CoreWeave told analysts it expects to keep parent-level debt financing under $5 billion through 2027. During the quarter, Nebius said it raised $5.75 billion in debt financing.

"Capital markets have been unsettled lately," CEO Arkady Volozh said on a conference call with analysts. "Two questions are being asked our whole industry, how fast can AI build-out happen, and who will pay for it? These are important questions. The way I think about it is simple. The demand for AI is real and substantial. Everything else, like building the capacity and financing it, is just a task. Tasks can be hard, but they all can be solved."

The political backdrop has created further volatility as Nebius races rivals to open up new data centers. Last week U.S. voters supported dozens of ballot initiatives to restrict the development of such facilities, while Democrats won sweeping victories in both houses of Congress.

#2971. Jensen and Lori Huang's Nvidia fortune is jointly controlled. How that makes things complicated now that he's resigned

A version of this article appeared in CNBC's Inside Wealth newsletter, a weekly guide to the high-net-worth investor and consumer. Sign up to receive future editions straight to your inbox.

Jensen Huang's $140 billion stake in Nvidia is largely held by trusts that are co-owned with his wife, Lori, creating added uncertainty for the shares and his financial future following his resignation from the company, according to attorneys and SEC filings.

Follow CNBC's live coverage of Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang's resignation

Huang and his wife jointly own about 800 million Nvidia shares worth roughly $135 billion through a complex web of trusts and LLCs created for tax and estate planning purposes, according to SEC filings. Jensen Huang directly owns about 70 million shares valued at about $12 billion.

While owning company stock through a trust is common among tech founders and CEOs, it's less typical for a founder to have equal ownership with a spouse, tax experts say. The arrangement further complicates Huang's sudden exit from the company, but could prove to be a stabilizing force for the shares, since the trusts make it more difficult for either Huang or his wife to quickly sell.

"Each of Mr. Huang and his wife may be deemed to have shared beneficial ownership of, and to exercise shared voting and investment power over," according to an SEC filing.

According to the filings, Jensen and Lori Huang own about 530 million shares as co-trustees of the Jen-Hsun and Lori Huang Living Trust, created in 1995. They're also co-trustees of the Huang 2012 Irrevocable Trust, which owns 31.4 million shares, and the Huang Irrevocable Remainder Trust, which owns 109 million shares. About 135 million shares are held by LLCs that have the Living Trust as the sole member, according to the filings.

Lori Huang is the president of the Jen-Hsun and Lori Huang Foundation, which owns 69 million shares and reported $9 billion in net assets in 2024, according to IRS filings.

Each of the trust structures serves different tax or estate purposes, according to trust and estate attorneys. The living trust is likely a revocable marital trust, since it was created 10 years after the Huangs married and two years after Nvidia was founded in 1993.  Attorneys say living trusts help couples avoid the potentially expensive and public probate process  when one spouse dies. Many couples, for example, create living trusts for homes they purchase or other assets.

The main tax benefit of living trusts is that when one spouse dies, the surviving spouse gets a step-up in basis for the entire trust's assets, rather than just their half, according to attorneys.

While the rules of each living trust vary, attorneys say shares in the living trust can typically be sold or divided fairly easily in the case of a divorce.

"It's like a joint checking account," said Kathleen Martone, partner at Varnum LLP.

While the revocable trust would typically allow each of them to sell their shares, lawyers said it is common for executives or their trusts to have agreements with the company limiting the sale or transfer of shares to prevent large-scale liquidation.

"In the event they divide shares in their revocable trust, there's probably transfer restrictions. He may have to hold them for her benefit, and she would get the monetary benefit of the shares but not voting or management rights," said Leslie Evans, partner at Withers. "He could retain the stock while Lori receives other assets or an equalization payment."

The more complicated structures used by the Huangs, and the ones with the largest tax benefits, are the irrevocable trusts. The SEC filings state that "Mr Huang and his wife are co-trustees" of both trusts. With an irrevocable trust, the creator transfers ownership of an asset into the trust and the terms can't be changed without the consent of the beneficiaries or a court.

Attorneys say the 2012 irrevocable trust is likely an "intentionally defective grantor trust," a type of irrevocable trust where the creator gifts assets but is still treated by the IRS as the owner of the property for income tax purposes.

The structure allows the creator to lend money to the trust to buy the shares, as well as pay income taxes for the trust when it earns money. The result — an unlimited gift to the trust that escapes the estate tax.

What's more, the trust can avoid capital gains taxes on any appreciation of the shares in the trust.

"The trust principal is staying high, my own taxable personal assets are being used to pay the tax, and I'm able to funnel more and more money into the trust," said Azriel Baer, trust and estates partner at Farrell Fritz, P.C. in New York.

Their other irrevocable trust, the "Huang Irrevocable Remainder Trust," was likely created for the benefit of their children.

By definition, the trustees and terms of an irrevocable trust are difficult to change. Since Jensen Huang and Lori Huang are both co-trustees, neither can likely remove or sell the Nvidia shares held by the trust, experts say.

"An irrevocable trust is its own entity that has its own operational provisions, and so it has some estate and asset protection planning," Martone said. "Theoretically an irrevocable trust would be protected from a divorcing spouse as a creditor."

Of course, the protections all depend on the terms of the trust, attorneys say. Whether an irrevocable trust can be changed or "decanted" in legal parlance depends in part on state law. California, where the Huangs live, is strict when it comes to changing irrevocable trusts.

For Nvidia shareholders, the irrevocable trusts could provide stability for the shares, since it would be difficult for either Jensen or Lori Huang to remove the shares from the trust and sell them.

Attorneys say it's unlikely the Huangs would change a trust created for the benefit of their children.

"If (the trusts) are for the benefit for their kids, there's no reason to pull them back into their estate," said Rochelle Schultz, shareholder at Weinstock Manion.

The Inside Wealth newsletter by Robert Frank is your weekly guide to high-net-worth investors and the industries that serve them.

#2969. Pistons vs. Wizards preview: Round two against the Wizards

The Pistons and Wizards play each other again, but it is after 3 days of rest for the Pistons and the day after a game for the Wizards because the NBA used Chat GPT to generate the schedule this year or something. This will only be the Pistons 3rd game in the last 8 days and their 2nd game against the Wizards.

The Pistons were able to control Monday's game from start-to-finish, but the game was a little closer than it should have been because they had no answer for Anthony Davis. Davis finished the game with 44 points and only missed 5 shots total.

The Pistons will still be without Jalen Duren, so they once again may struggle to guard Anthony Davis. But, as Monday proved, even if they can't stop Davis, they can still win if they stop the rest of the team.

Where: Capital One Arena in Washington DCWhen: Thursday, November 12 at 7 pm ESTWatch: Scripps Sports NetworkOdds: Pistons (-10.5)

There isn't a ton more to say about this matchup that wasn't very evident in Monday's game. With Jalen Duren out, the Pistons have no solution to opposing big men with any kind of offensive skill that are over 6'10". It seems hyperbolic, but it really has been the case. Paul Reed is undersized and John Collins is not a natural center. Tolu Smith has the size to possibly keep up, but he has proven to be out of his element pretty much any time he has gotten playing time so far.

The Pistons did opt to start John Collins at center against the Wizards on Monday and it did work out offensively, as he had a season-high 24 points. But, the aforementioned Anthony Davis was able to cruise to 44 points without much resistance. Anthony Davis always seems to have his best games against the Pistons and I wouldn't expect him to hit a couple threes every single night, but a lot of the offense he was able to generate was off of offensive rebound putbacks or shots over the undersized players guarding him.

Surprisingly, the Pistons were only outrebounded 46-45, but they were outrebounded 14-11 on the offensive glass. If you watched the game, you could see the Pistons lack of size rear it's head countless times. It is just the current state of things and something the Pistons will have to manage as best as they can for the next two weeks while Jalen Duren is still out with his hamstring injury.

The Pistons benefitted heavily from poor games out of Alex Sarr and Kyshawn George, which George in particular has been arguably the Wizards 2nd best player this season. Trae Young also had a poor night from beyond the arc where he shot 3-of-10. Some of that is because of how good the Pistons perimeter defense is and if the Pistons can replicate that tonight, it doesn't really matter if they have nobody to slow down Anthony Davis.

AJ Dybantsa was able to get going a bit on Monday as he scored 21 points on 8-of-17 shooting. There were times where he looked overwhelmed, as he had 5 turnovers, but he showed why he was the number 1 pick and a building block for the Wizards. The Pistons have perimeter players to slow him down, but the refs were very whistle-happy on Monday which helped Dybantsa get momentum on his drives to the basket since the Pistons were not able to be as physical.

One small point of concern has been the play of Cade Cunningham over the last few games. He had arguably his best game of the season a little over a week ago against the Charlotte Hornets and since then has really struggled, especially from beyond the arc. He is 1 for his last 19 from beyond the arc after being close to 40% through the first seven games of the season.

He is playing more off-ball and getting clean looks, they just aren't falling right now. Teams are obviously focusing their defense more on Cade, but a lot of the current issues are just due to not being able to hit his outside shots. Hopefully that changes tonight.

Luckily, Daniss Jenkins has been able to step up the last couple nights as he had a career-high, 31 points against the Lakers on Sunday and then 22 points, 6 rebounds, and 8 assists on Monday while starting. Given the success the Pistons had starting him along with John Collins at center, I would suspect that they roll out that starting lineup again.

There were some issues defensively, which I already touched on, but the offense was much better with Collins starting and more-importantly, it finally got John Collins going after his early-season struggles.

If the Pistons are able to replicate the game they played on Monday, they shouldn't have a difficult time defeating the Wizards, but if they let one of the other players besides Anthony Davis get hot, the Wizards have a shot at the upset.

Detroit Pistons (7-3): Cade Cunningham, Daniss Jenkins, Duncan Robinson, Ausar Thompson, John Collins

Washington Wizards (4-8): Trae Young, Kyshawn George, AJ Dybantsa, Anthony Davis, Alex Sarr

Do you think there is a solution to the Pistons big-man struggles or do they just need to ride it out until Jalen Duren returns?

#2965. ‘A dark day for Nvidia’: Here’s what analysts have been saying about Jensen Huang’s departure

Nvidia investors are preparing for a future without Jensen Huang after the CEO's resignation was announced on Thursday.

Markets were on tenterhooks ahead of the decision amid reports that the chip giant's board had convened to discuss his future with the company. By the time of the announcement, at least six analysts had downgraded the stock between Wednesday night and Thursday morning.

The New York Times on Wednesday reported that Huang had engaged in a second extramarital affair with an employee. The CEO was already under scrutiny after Huang admitted to having a romantic relationship with a woman U.S. intelligence believes is a Chinese spy.

Nvidia announced on Thursday morning that, following a board meeting, Huang would step down after more than 30 years at the company's helm.

CFO Colette Kress has been named interim CEO.

Those downgrading the stock included longtime Nvidia bull Ben Reitzes of Melius Research.

Nvidia shares were seen down more than 12% shortly after the open on Wall Street, bringing losses for the last month close to 25%.

Here's what analysts and investors are saying about the company and the stock.

Reitzes changed his rating for Nvidia to hold from buy. His price target is now $185 — down from $315 — is 6% above the level at which shares last closed.

"We thought we had experience, but nothing prepares you for what has happened at Nvidia over the last few weeks - and especially since Saturday. The only situation remotely close to this we can recall was HP in 2010. Waiting to see what that Board would do (and who they would hire) that time didn't work out. It was really cheap, too, at ~9x EPS right after its initial swoon. Nvidia is a much stronger, more important company, but its market moves far faster, and this story touches geopolitical flashpoints. In this unique crisis, we have decided (reluctantly) to step aside on Nvidia (now a Hold) after recommending it since we launched our AI platform coverage universe in July 2023," he said.

Analyst Mark Lipacis downgraded Nvidia to in line from outperform. He also lowered his price target on shares to $186 from $465, implying 7% upside from Wednesday's close.

"The Wall Street Journal reported Nvidia's Board was 'preparing to oust' its CEO after he had two extramarital affairs, including one with a suspected Chinese spy. The NY Times reported that Nvidia's Board hired law firm Wachtell Lipton to lead an investigation into the matter. While Nvidia's Board said that it 'has no reason to believe that either national security or Nvidia secrets were compromised,' we believe that the investigation will take time and we expect NVDA's P/E multiple will continue to compress consistent with our 'Late-Innings of the Semi Cycle' thesis, especially since Nvidia has no obvious succession plan or number 2 operational executive," he said.

William Blair's Sebastien Naji downgraded Nvidia to market perform from outperform.

"After a head-spinning week of news and allegations around CEO Jensen Huang, Nvidia's board is reportedly preparing to remove him, according to the Wall Street Journal. The full story will take time to emerge and may require several investigations, including the board's own independent probe and a requested U.S. House briefing. What is already clear is that Huang's likely departure and heightened scrutiny of the company will be a significant overhang on NVDA shares in the near term. Meanwhile, the potential for a slower AI buildout and more intense competition from other chipmakers will only add to execution risk...governance and regulatory pressure, the potential for more serious fallout, and a sudden change in management are likely to muddle both execution and sentiment. As a result, we are downgrading NVDA to Market Perform and will wait for the dust to settle before reassessing," he said.

Analyst Rick Schafer downgraded the AI stock to perform from outperform.

"We are downgrading shares of NVDA and removing our price target following reports (WSJ, NYT) of founding CEO Jensen Huang's imminent departure amidst allegations of a 2nd extramarital affair. Credible media reports circulated Wednesday evening that NVDA's board is preparing to part ways with Huang following a widely reported relationship with an alleged Chinese spy compounded by new allegations of a relationship with an NVDA executive. This 'one-two' punch appears to have forced the board's hand...While we see upside to the F3Q (Oct.) print and F4Q (Jan.) outlook, we expect fundamentals to take a backseat near term. The departure of Mr. Huang could, in our view, translate to a lingering share overhang," he said.

Analyst Stacy Rasgon slashed his price target from $400 and downgraded the stock from market outperform.

"So now what? On the positive, the fundamentals of the business (if not the leadership) areclearly still intact with numbers that remain massive (and probably still headed higher). We don't think the company should have huge issues operating while engaged in a search for new leadership. And (of course) the shares are almost absurdly cheap (and getting cheaper as we speak); clearly there is a lot of bad news getting priced in here," he said.

"On the other hand, we find it hard to advocate stepping in here in size as there will no doubt be more allegations to come (there is a trope that says if you find two ants in your kitchen the best estimate of the total number of ants in your kitchen is not two...), both from internal issues as well as headlines related to the business, new governmental investigations etc. Adding on the new variable of who will take the reins (internal? external?) and anything else they may or may not uncover creates additional uncertainty (and we can't even begin to speculate as to who they might find to fill Jensen's shoes)," he added.

Analyst John Vinh downgraded the stock from overweight.

"We believe Huang's departure represents a material increase in strategic, organizational, and execution risk that warrants a higher risk premium and lower valuation multiple for the stock. While we expect core fundamentals to remain strong for the foreseeable future, we believe it will be challenging for NVDA to find a successor with the same leadership and strategic vision as Huang," he said.

"In our view the writing is on the wall that Jensen's days at Nvidia could be numbered and we would expect this to be a very fluid situation over the next 48 hours," renowned tech bull Dan Ives said in a note overnight.

"Jensen has created a black eye moment for himself and Nvidia and now has created a spider web of complexity that Wall Street, Silicon Valley, DC, and the global tech ecosystem are trying to figure out what is next."

"We believe Nvidia's value is $300 per share with Jensen as CEO and $225 per share without Jensen as CEO," Ives added.

"The stock will clearly be under pressure with this fluid news but the reality is its a dark day for Nvidia, but the company will navigate this turmoil and get through to the other side and remain a core foundational piece of the AI Revolution."

Citi analysts said overnight that they had trimmed their target price for Nvidia to $225, down from $285.

"If Jensen were forced out, it would mark the end of an era," Malik and Sylla said in their note.

"Under his leadership, Nvidia became the dominant force in AI infrastructure, the primary beneficiary of the global AI spending cycle, and one of the world's most valuable companies. A leadership transition creates an overhang on the stock given his central role in strategy, product vision, customer relationships, and investor confidence."

They said Nvidia CFO Colette Kress could serve as interim CEO while the board evaluates external candidates.

"As much as there is a lesson here for Nvidia around corporate governance, around key man risk… the reality is there is still 40,000 other employees at Nvidia, and there is some real talent there, and I have no doubt that there are some executors within Nvidia that the board will be able to tap to execute on their existing plans and continue to manage the company," Nathan Thooft, the firm's CIO, told CNBC's "Squawk Box Asia" on Thursday.

"I think the bigger risk is the foresight in the next several years, where Nvidia was driven by a lot of the mindset and thoughts that come out of their CEO, and if he's no longer there they're going to have to find that replacement."

"You can't deny the board has done a horrible job of succession planning," Greifeld told CNBC's "Morning Call" on Thursday.

"What the board has to find out in the next days or weeks is, did Jensen lie to them? Right? Did he lie to them repeatedly on different topics? We don't know. Does he lose their trust basically on lying to them over the years? I'm sure the board would love to keep Jensen in place in some role. Right, his value to the company is quite large."

Greifeld said he does not see a premium in Nvidia stock, noting that it trades at a low multiple "so there's no bubble to burst here with respect to the valuation."

"If there's a new CEO in, they don't have to make any decisions day one, day 30, day 60. They have time to really get their footing and come up with their own strategic vision," he told CNBC. "You're not going to change what is in the development plan for chips that exists today. So I think certainly it would be a loss, but given the nature of the business and the long lead time for these chip developments and the long contractual relationships they have with customers, it would give them certainly room to maneuver."

CIO Richard Reyle told CNBC's "Squawk Box Asia" that Nvidia's board had painted themselves into a corner by not having any kind of succession plan in place.

"I've heard him compared to Steve Jobs being removed from Apple, but [Jobs] put in place a succession plan and cultivated and groomed Tim Cook for years, so that when he passed away and [Cook] took over, there was a real leader there," he said. "Right now, Nvidia doesn't have that person in place, so it's going to be a choice of keeping Jensen and being faced with … all kinds of interrogations, or they're going to have to move on from him, and that's just going to make the stock uninvestable. That doesn't make the AI trade uninvestable, but it certainly makes Nvidia uninvestable."

Reyle said he saw the stock moving "lower and much lower from here."

"There's no obvious [replacement] there that can step right in and take over, so they may kind of try to ride this out and retain him," he said. "But there's going to be a lot of difficulty in doing so because he's going to become something of a liability."

"NVIDIA upheaval puts tech concentration risks in focus," Mark Haefele, global wealth management chief investment officer at UBS, and his team said on Thursday morning.

"Without taking any views on individual companies, the episode reinforces the importance of investment diversification and selectivity as the AI trade matures. We retain our conviction in AI's long-term potential, with compute demand remaining robust and earnings resilient. But elevated expectations and potentially wide differences in company outcomes can leave concentrated portfolios exposed to company-specific setbacks."

"Jensen Huang is toast," Bill Blain, founder and principal, said in his morning newsletter. "Nvidia's dependence on Jensen Huang is now exposed as a major market risk. The dominant firm within the AI industry lacks clear succession planning and Huang was seen as the acceptable face of AI by the public."

"Confidence in the AI sector has been wobbling for months," he added. "Huang's departure could lead to instability and trigger another collapse."

#2963. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 stocks for their solid growth potential

Investors are closely monitoring earnings releases of artificial intelligence companies amid concerns about the durability of high spending and demand. While quarterly results provide key insights into a company's performance and execution, it is prudent to focus on long-term prospects and look beyond short-term fluctuations.

Top Wall Street analysts can help investors pick stocks with attractive long-term prospects as their assessments are backed by in-depth research of a company's fundamentals and growth opportunities.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

AI-powered data analytics provider Palantir Technologies is this week's first pick. The company reported market-beating second-quarter results. Moreover, Palantir raised its full-year guidance and now expects U.S. commercial business revenue to grow by at least 134%.

Following the Q2 print, Bank of America analyst Mariana Perez Mora reiterated a buy rating on PLTR stock with a price target of $255. Mora said that Palantir's performance reflects the success of its AI strategy, which focuses on delivering the right infrastructure to customers and ensuring better outcomes.

"The main driver continues to be the U.S. commercial business, which now represents almost 40% of total (vs. 30% a year ago and just 20% a couple of years back)," noted Mora.

The five-star analyst highlighted the 149% growth in Palantir's U.S. commercial business in Q2 2026 and the expansion in its customer base and engagement levels, with clients pursuing more integrated relationships. Mora noted that the U.S. commercial customer count increased 35% year-over-year to 653, with trailing twelve months revenue per customer surging 76% to $3.5 million in the second quarter.

Citing strength across Palantir's commercial and government segments, Mora increased her sales and earnings per share estimates for 2026-2028. The analyst attributed the increase in her estimates to the growth in the commercial business, backed by stronger total contract value in the second quarter.

Mora ranks No. 444 among more than 12,400 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 32.6%. See Palantir Insider Trading Activity on TipRanks.

E-commerce and cloud computing giant Amazon impressed investors with its strong second-quarter results. Revenue from the company's Amazon Web Services (AWS) cloud business surged 37%, marking the unit's fastest growth since 2021.

In reaction to the Q2 2026 performance, JPMorgan analyst Doug Anmuth reiterated a buy rating on Amazon stock and raised his price target to $365 from $330, saying that it remains the firm's Best Idea. Anmuth highlighted that the company's forex-neutral top-line growth accelerated to 20% in Q2 2026, the fastest in 20 quarters. He added that the top line gained from acceleration across both AWS and stores.

The five-star analyst noted that AWS growth of 37% was the fastest in 18 quarters, with backlog jumping nearly 2.5x year-over-year and 36% sequentially to $496 billion. Amazon's backlog gained from robust demand across both core workloads and AI adoption. Anmuth also observed the strength in AWS' AI and chips businesses, Graviton revenue commitments, and AWS' core business.

Notably, Anmuth believes that the core AWS business is highly correlated to AI revenue, and he expects further strengthening of this relationship as more AI workloads move into full-scale production and fuel higher demand. Overall, the analyst is more optimistic on Amazon following Q2 results and raised his 2026 and 2027 sales estimates by about 1% to 2% and operating income by about 5% to 7%.

"Importantly, management emphasized that the [return on invested capital] on its AI investments is compelling and it has a clear line-of-sight to strong financial returns," said Anmuth.

Anmuth ranks No. 717 among more than 12,400 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.5%. See Amazon Statistics on TipRanks.

Moving on to Lam Research, a supplier of semiconductor manufacturing equipment and services. Thanks to AI-driven demand, the company delivered better-than-anticipated fiscal fourth quarter results.

In reaction to the solid results, Oppenheimer analyst Edward Yang reaffirmed a buy rating on LRCX stock with a price target of $400. Yang stated that Lam Research exceeded June-quarter expectations despite the high bar, driven by strong performance of the Customer Support Business Group and doubling of NAND revenue from the prior quarter. He also cited the improvement in Q4 FY26 gross margin.

The five-star analyst highlighted that Lam Research's September-quarter revenue and EPS outlook exceeded the Street's expectations by mid-teens percentages. Yang added that the company increased its wafer fabrication equipment spending outlook to the low-$150 billion range, up from its prior $140 billion estimate.

Importantly, Yang noted that Lam Research called calendar year 2027 "extraordinary," citing persistent supply shortages and the addition of 8 to 10 new fabrication plants. The analyst raised his 2027 revenue and EPS estimates by 7% and 9% to $33 billion and $8.88, respectively. Yang also increased his 2028 revenue and EPS estimates by 7% and 8% to $36.6 billion and $9.89, respectively.

"It's the cleanest way to play AI-driven 3D scaling across memory, foundry/logic, and packaging," said Yang about LRCX.

Yang ranks No. 256 among more than 12,400 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 71% of the time, delivering an average return of 56.4%. See Lam Research Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

An official website of the United States Government
