#2875. Lawson warned Red Bull exit is the only way to fulfilling F1 title dream

Liam Lawson may have secured his Formula 1 future with Racing Bulls for 2027, but former Haas team principal Guenther Steiner believes the New Zealander must eventually leave the Red Bull family if he wants to become a world champion.

Racing Bulls officially confirmed on Thursday that Lawson will remain with the team next season alongside Arvid Lindblad, ending months of speculation over his future.

The announcement comes after an impressive 2026 campaign for the 24-year-old, who has established himself as the leading midfield driver and helped Racing Bulls strengthen their grip on fifth place in the Constructors' Championship.

However, Steiner believes Lawson's ambitions should extend beyond simply retaining his seat, suggesting that his chances of returning to the senior Red Bull team are extremely limited.

Speaking to New Zealand radio station More FM before Thursday's announcement, the former Haas boss correctly predicted that Racing Bulls would retain Lawson for another season.

"Firstly, yes I think they will stick with him next year. And then I think Liam needs to make his own way out of the Red Bull family. I think his promotion back to the Red Bull team from Racing Bulls, I don't think that will happen. And if you don't go there, he cannot be world champion."

Lawson's relationship with the senior Red Bull team has already been complicated. After earning a promotion for the start of the 2025 season, he was demoted to Racing Bulls after just two Grands Prix.

He subsequently returned to Red Bull for three races in 2026, replacing the injured Isack Hadjar, but the Frenchman's confirmation alongside Max Verstappen for 2027 has effectively closed the door on another immediate promotion.

Nevertheless, Steiner believes Lawson possesses the necessary talent to challenge for a world championship, provided he eventually secures a seat with a leading team.

"Has he got the talent to be world champion? I think in the right car, he can be. There are a lot of them with similar talent like him at the moment in Formula 1. But when you get in the right team at the right place at the right time, he could achieve it."

Lawson's performances have provided a strong argument for his retention, with the New Zealander scoring points in 15 of the opening 20 Grands Prix this season.

His latest result came in Brazil, where he started third and briefly ran second before eventually finishing seventh following Charles Leclerc's five-second penalty.

Steiner believes those performances demonstrate that Lawson deserves an opportunity to compete with Formula 1's leading drivers: "I think he has shown in this moment that he can do a very good job with Racing Bulls, who are not in the top four cars, but he's always the best of the rest. He shows he can do a good job, but now he needs to be in the big machines and at one of the top four teams."

#2873. Sixers, Brown have a ‘humbling' night in loss to Celtics

Sixers, Brown have a 'humbling' night in loss to Celtics originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Whatever Jaylen Brown and the Sixers dreamed up for their first matchup against the Celtics soon flew out the window on Tuesday night.

Brown was whistled for two early fouls, the Sixers fell behind and their early-season flaws were on full display in a 123-101 loss at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

"We've just got to get better," Brown said. "Tonight was humbling. We got outplayed, out-executed. So we go back to the drawing board and get ready for the next one."

Over 26 minutes, Brown had 13 points on 4-for-9 shooting and five rebounds against the team that traded him away this summer.

As for the endless dissection of his relationship with former co-star Jayson Tatum, Brown didn't have much to add Tuesday. There was no public reconciliation, no obvious on-court animosity and generally nothing to satisfy fans hoping for some kind of fireworks on that front.

"I'm just focused on doing what's best for my team," Brown said. "Everything else … to be honest, I wasn't paying attention. But credit to Jayson; he played well tonight. Credit to the Celtics. They executed, they shot the hell out of the ball and they were the better team tonight."

Tatum scored 21 points, two of which came on a third-quarter slam over LeBron James. Paul George continued his fantastic start as a Celtic by dropping 36 points and draining six three-pointers.

The Celtics dominated from beyond the arc, shooting 22 for 49 (44.9 percent). The Sixers went just 11 for 37 (29.7 percent) on another night of underwhelming offense. The team sits 22nd in the NBA in offensive rating, 24th in three-point makes per game and 21st in three-point percentage.

"I think that we've got some guys that are obviously shooting much lower percentages than their last season or their career average — almost a handful of guys," Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said. "And I don't think it's because they're shooting fallaway, totally contested ones. I think they're shots we want them to take. So I think they'll continue to take 'em and I'm pretty sure those will even out into their career averages."

Joel Embiid is at 37 percent from the floor and 4 for 33 from three-point range (12.1 percent).

The Sixers' seven-time All-Star center got through an injury scare when he rolled his ankle late in the second quarter and hit a three early in the third. It was his first long-range make since the Sixers' home opener back on Oct. 22.

"It's a work in progress," Embiid said. "I think the biggest thing is just not thinking. I'm probably overthinking it. The rhythm of the game is different. I'm so used to having to create for myself, so it's a little bit different.

"But I think I'm slowly getting better. You could see as soon as I made a three, the next shot in the short corner … I didn't even think about it. I just shot it and let it fly, and I made it. … I think it's just about taking what's in front of you. Make or miss, keep working on your game. It's going to get there at some point."

Even with the Sixers second in the Eastern Conference standings at 9-3, Embiid believes the team's defense has "slacked off a little bit" in recent games.

"I don't really look at the record," he said. "I just look at the process of, 'Are we getting better every day?' We can always do a better job at all phases of the game."

"They're one of the top teams in the league and they've been together for a while," Brown said. "We've got a lot to work on over here, so we've got to get to work."

The Sixers will face the Raptors on Thursday night in Philadelphia.

They'll have to wait until Jan. 21 in Boston to see the Celtics again.

"I was looking forward to this," Brown said. "I was looking forward to playing well, but s— happens. Just get ready for the next one."

Here's how the company did compared to LSEG consensus estimates:

For the fiscal second quarter, Cisco said it expects revenue of between $18 billion and $18.2 billion, topping the average analyst estimate of $17.88 billion, according to LSEG. The company is projecting adjusted earnings per share of $1.34 to $1.36, beating the $1.23 average estimate.

#2871. Michael Fasusi talks biggest change Jason Witten made to the offense

The Oklahoma Sooners are coming off one of their better offensive games of the season against the Florida Gators. The numbers aren't staggering, as Oklahoma totaled 351 yards of total offense, including 207 in the air and 144 on the ground.

But anyone who has watched Oklahoma this season could tell there was something different about the unit in Gainesville. Interim offensive coordinator Jason Witten gets the bulk of the credit. He replaced offensive coordinator Ben Arbuckle as play caller after Arbuckle was fired earlier in the week.

Oklahoma star left tackle Michael Fasusi praised the mindset Witten instilled in the offense.

"There's no special scheme or magic play; it just comes down to us executing the plays for real," Fasusi said via SoonerScoop. "I've just got to give a lot of credit to Coach Witten for just his knowledge of the game. The mindset that he has and just the mindset that he continues to instill in us to just literally go out there and just play ball. Don't think, just play."

That simpler approach could be exactly what Oklahoma needed after an offense that too often looked hesitant and out of sync. Rather than overcomplicating things, Witten appears focused on getting players to trust their preparation, play with confidence and execute their assignments. Fasusi's comments offer a glimpse into how that message has resonated with the offensive line.

The real test will be whether the Sooners can carry that mentality into their matchup with the Ole Miss Rebels. The Rebels presented a talented defense, and Oklahoma will need another disciplined performance up front to establish the run and give its passing game room to operate. If the offensive line continues to play with the confidence it showed against Florida, the Sooners can build on their progress and give Witten another strong outing as play caller.

#2870. Top Wall Street analysts are upbeat about the prospects for these 3 stocks

September ended on a mixed note, with the S&P 500 and Dow Jones Industrial Average falling 0.5% and 4.3%, respectively, while the Nasdaq Composite climbed 1.9%. Markets remained on edge with higher oil prices and Treasury yields weighing on investor sentiment and triggering fears of additional interest rate hikes.

In this climate, investors with a long-term horizon can track recommendations from top Wall Street analysts to find stocks with solid growth prospects.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Neocloud company CoreWeave (CRWV) is benefiting from robust AI-driven demand for cloud computing.

Recently, JPMorgan analyst Samik Chatterjee upgraded CoreWeave to buy from hold, raising his price target to $125 from $120. The analyst cited favorable pricing, driven by solid demand, for the upgrade.

Chatterjee said CoreWeave is now more willing to sign premium priced, shorter-term contracts, which lift his revenue and margin estimates. CRWV stock performance has been underwhelming as investors worry about high capital spending, the analyst noted. That said, he believes that higher prices and increased margins will more than offset the rise in debt to fund capital spending, improving the stock's valuation.

Chatterjee noted the 25% price hike in July across CoreWeave's product offerings and frequent price increases by rivals like Nebius (NBIS), adding that some peers are charging nearly three times more for short-term compute contracts than what CoreWeave charges on longer-term deals.

"CoreWeave's leverage of the opportunity [is] evident in its recent press release highlighting contracts signed in F3Q at ~$40M/MW," said Chatterjee. JPMorgan said higher pricing is already contributing 5-10 percentage points to contract margins versus earlier deals.

Chatterjee ranks No. 4 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 75% of the time, delivering an average return of 55.3%. See CoreWeave Statistics on TipRanks.

Palo Alto Networks (PANW) is a provider of cybersecurity solutions across network, cloud, security operations, AI and identity. Following a discussion with management, BTIG analyst Gray Powell reiterated a buy rating on PANW and raised his price target to $425 from $404.

After a review of Palo Alto's product-level growth catalysts, Powell sees clear upside to both revenue and next-generation security (NGS) annual recurring revenue (ARR) in fiscal 2027. He expects PANW to deliver pro forma revenue growth of more than 17%, above the consensus estimate of 14.6%, and NGS ARR growth of more than 26% versus the Street's expectation of 24.5%.

Over the past five years, the Palo Alto business mix, has moved to higher-growth product areas and new markets. from what was earlier viewed as a hardware-centric network security company, Powell said. The 5-star analyst estimates that hardware firewalls and attached subscriptions now account for less than 50% of revenue.

Meanwhile, high-growth solutions, including Prisma SASE, software firewalls, Cortex XSIAM, Chronosphere and Idira now represent more than 35% of PANW's revenue. "Investors may not fully realize the growth potential within PANW's platform story," Powell said.

BTIG is optimistic about the prospects of Chronosphere in observability, CyberArk in identity and software firewalls and Prisma AIRS in the network and AI security business.

Powell ranks No. 320 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of 17.3%. See Palo Alto Networks KPIs on TipRanks.

In a research note on September 30, Rosenblatt analyst Scott Devitt reiterated a buy rating on e-commerce and cloud computing platform Amazon (AMZN), raising his price target to $360 from $335. Fears that agentic commerce poses a major threat to Amazon's advertising business seem "overstated," he said.

Devitt noted that the growing adoption of personal agents and AI shopping assistants has sparked concern about displacement of Amazon's retail media business. But the 5-star analyst remains bullish, given Amazon's ability to adapt to changing consumer behavior, as it's done in the past.

Additionally, Devitt argues that even if an AI agent helps a shopper discover a product, Amazon can still handle the purchase and delivery, keeping much of the transaction value. He highlighted that management believes that existing Amazon shoppers will likely continue to start their shopping journey on the platform because it has better personalization, accurate pricing and inventory information that third-party agents still lack.

Overall, Devitt expects Amazon's retail business to prove a net beneficiary of agentic commerce. Fewer clicks would not hurt the company if the value of each remaining click and overall volumes keep rising, he said. The argument is that even if Amazon loses some sponsored ad revenue over the next few years, it would likely be only a small part of the company's total business and could be offset by growth in other areas.

"Consistent with our AI framework, we continue to view Amazon as one of the most resilient leaders in our coverage, combining owned compute infrastructure with a physical fulfillment moat," said Devitt. His bullish view is supported, Devitt said, by expectations that Amazon Web Services (AWS) will exit the year at a more than 45% growth rate with continued margin improvement.

Devitt ranks No. 765 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 49% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Amazon Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Houser left the game late in the third quarter with Illinois leading 23-10. Clayton completed 6 of 8 passes for 88 yards and two touchdowns. The final touchdown came in overtime on a 25-yard pass that put Illinois up 38-31.

Sophomore quarterback Carson Boyd also played. He rushed once for seven yards but did not attempt a pass.

Bielema and offensive coordinator Barry Lunney Jr. both said Boyd could play against Oregon (4-2, 2-1), but Clayton is the starter.

Usually, Bielema said, his starting quarterback gets a lot of rest during the bye week while the staff works on player development. Given Clayton and Boyd's inexperience, both got valuable reps in practice last week.

"I think those two guys in particular took advantage of every rep," Bielema said.

Bielema has leaned heavily on transfer quarterbacks during his six years at Illinois, including Houser. But that could change if Clayton, a former three-star recruit, earns the job.

"I've said several times that hopefully the next starter comes from within our program," Bielema said.

Lunney said Clayton will be more prepared than he has in the past given the circumstances.

Michigan State Spartans defensive back Charles Brantley (4) tackles Illinois Fighting Illini wide receiver Collin Dixon (17) in a Big 10 football game at Spartan Stadium in East Lansing, Mich. on Saturday, October 10, 2026.Isaac Ritchey | iritchey@mlive.com

"He knows he's going to play now," Lunney told reporters.

"We feel like we have another guy on campus in that room that can help us win and we're very confident in that," Bielema said.

Oregon coach Dan Lanning said it's difficult to scout Clayton given his limited body of work. Oregon has watched some of his high school game film. But mostly, Oregon will focus on Illinois' system.

"There might be wrinkles, but very rarely do people change the identity of their offense based on a change of the position," Lanning said. "So you prepare and then you make sure you have answers for what you think that skill set is."

If Moore doesn't return this week, Raiola and Thomas will compete for the starting job against Illinois.

Raiola replaced Moore at USC and thrived in the win, but following a bye, struggled for three quarters during a 17-15 loss to UCLA on Oct. 10. Thomas performed better in the fourth quarter and almost pulled out the victory.

Thomas started Saturday's 17-14 home win over Nebraska, but struggled for three quarters before Lanning turned to Raiola. He led the Ducks from a 14-6 deficit to a win with a four-yard touchdown pass to tight end Jamari Johnson with 28 seconds remaining.

Lanning said he's confident in all four quarterbacks, including sophomore Akili Smith Jr., who had a play package ready for Nebraska if needed.

Illinois is most familiar with Raiola, who, as a Nebraska freshman in 2024, passed for 297 yards, three touchdowns and one interception in a 31-24 overtime loss to Illinois.

"Raiola is obviously a very accomplished player," Bielema said. "Kind of almost unusual. You got to almost prepare for possibly three quarterbacks on Saturday. But we'll kind of handle that during the course of the week and see where that goes."

Oregon (4-2, 2-1 Big Ten) at Illinois (3-3, 1-2)

Where: Gies Memorial Stadium, Champaign, Ill.

#2865. Trump says ‘we don’t need a deal with Iran’

The U.S. should no longer seek a deal with Iran, President Donald Trump said Thursday as he directed Vice President JD Vance to instead focus his attention on corruption and crime in Democratic cities and states.

"We don't need a deal with Iran. Those days have long since passed," Trump wrote in a Truth Social post.

"We've got their money, Hundreds of Billions of Dollars, and we've got their Strait!" Trump wrote, referring to the Strait of Hormuz, a key choke point in the ongoing U.S.-Iran war.

The announcement came just over a week after Republicans suffered huge losses in the midterm election. Vance and other Republicans have pinned those losses on the Iran war, which the U.S. and Israel started in late February.

In the weeks leading up to Election Day, Trump repeatedly suggested the U.S. could resume launching military strikes, which have largely subsided as a tense stalemate has taken hold in the region.

The Wall Street Journal on Wednesday reported that Vance, who has led diplomatic efforts with Iran in prior months, was considering face-to-face talks with a top Iranian security official.

Stock futures declined following Trump's post.

The unpopular Iran war, which Trump and others in his administration said would be won in a matter of weeks, is now in its ninth month with no clear end in sight. The conflict has driven up global energy prices, creating a ripple effect through the economy and exacerbating concerns about inflation and affordability that were paramount to voters in the midterms.

Corruption emerged as another top issue that broke in Democrats' favor in the election. Trump's post Thursday morning attempts to flip the script, blaming Democratic-run areas for allowing corruption and crime to proliferate.

"I have asked Vice President Vance to focus his complete and total attention on the Massive Corruption and Crime taking place in Democrat run Cities and States throughout the U.S.," Trump wrote.

"The Blue States, such as Minnesota, California, Illinois, and many more, are all poorly run, corrupt disasters. Billions of Dollars is being stolen each and every month," the president wrote. "Our Budget could be quickly BALANCED with the correction of this large scale criminal activity."

"CORRUPTION is in the Democrat Leadership's blood!" he added.

#2864. IRS announces 2027 401(k) contribution limits, raises savings cap: What it means for your money

The IRS has announced new retirement account contribution limits for 2027.

In its release on Thursday, the agency increased the 401(k) employee deferral limit to $25,500 next year, up from $24,500 in 2026. The change applies to 401(k)s, 403(b)s and most 457 plans, along with the federal Thrift Savings Plan.

The IRS also unveiled 2027 catch-up contribution limits for savers age 50 and older, new individual retirement account savings limits and higher income thresholds for Roth IRA contributions.

Starting in 2027, the 401(k) catch-up contribution limit will rise to $8,500 for savers 50 and older, up from $8,000 in 2026. But investors aged 60 to 63 can instead save an extra $11,750, based on changes enacted via Secure 2.0. That figure is up $500 from 2026.

Both amounts are in addition to the $25,500 deferral limit for 2027.

The 2027 IRA contribution limit is $7,500, unchanged from 2026, the IRS announced. IRA catch-up contributions for investors age 50 and older will be $1,100, the same as 2026.

The announcement comes one week after the agency released dozens of other inflation adjustments for 2027, including federal income tax brackets, higher capital gains brackets, estate and gift tax exemptions and provisions impacting families, among others.

In 2025, only 14% of participants maxed out their 401(k)s, according to Vanguard's 2026 How America Saves report, which was based on data from more than 1,300 qualified plans and nearly 5 million participants. The average combined savings rate, including employer deposits, was an estimated 12.1%, according to the same report.

"Before worrying about contribution limits, make sure you're capturing your full employer match," said Sean Michael Pearson, a certified financial planner with Ameriprise Financial Services in Conshohocken, Pennsylvania.

During the second quarter of 2026, about 19% of employees weren't deferring enough to 401(k)s to receive their company's full matching contribution, according to a Fidelity analysis of 27,300 corporate plans and nearly 26 million participants.

Vanguard suggests saving 12% to 15% of your pay per year, including employer contributions, depending on your income. But only one-quarter of 401(k) participants deferred more than 10% of earnings in 2025, according to Vanguard's report.

If you plan to max out your 401(k) for 2027, you should change your savings rate in December so it's active by your first January paycheck, experts say. The update could take one or two pay periods to go into effect.

The "most important habit" is consistently increasing your 401(k) savings rate over time, according to Pearson.

For example, if you bump 401(k) contributions by 1% every year, "you may barely notice the difference in your paycheck, but the long-term impact can be substantial," he said.

In 2025, about 27% of 401(k) plans automatically increased employee deferrals over time, known as "auto-escalation," according to the Plan Sponsor Council of America's yearly survey of 401(k)s. The nonprofit trade organization's survey included 753 plans.

The vast majority of plans with auto-escalation, 69%, boosted deferrals by 1% per year, the Plan Sponsor Council of America found.

#2863. Widespread Microsoft outage takes down Outlook and other 365 services

Microsoft suffered a widespread outage on Thursday that impacted thousands of users across regions, according to outage-tracking website Downdetector.com.

The outage began shortly before 1 p.m. and impacted Microsoft 365 services, Outlook and Teams. At the time, about 130,000 users reported issues with Outlook.

Amazon Web Services and Google users also reported outages on the site.

"We are aware and investigating reports of users experiencing issues with Microsoft 365 services, primarily issues with Exchange Online," a Microsoft spokesperson wrote in a statement to CNBC. "We appreciate our customers' patience while we work to restore services as soon as possible."

Microsoft shared updates on the outage from its Microsoft 365 Status account on social media platform X.

Amazon wrote on X that services were "operating normally" and an "unrelated disruption elsewhere on the internet is prompting some inaccurate news reports." The company referred users to its health dashboard for "accurate data."

At 4 p.m., Microsoft said it had "validated an alternative service health check method" that brought some affected servers back online.

"We're now applying this change more broadly," Microsoft wrote. "We're also preparing automated recovery systems to continually detect and restart unresponsive servers, to maintain service stability while this change progresses."

Microsoft initially said infrastructure "elevated load across multiple regions" was responsible for the connectivity problems.

The company later determined that the elevated load was caused by a "number of servers being unexpectedly unavailable; straining the remaining infrastructure" and that some servers were failing automated health checks.

"We've taken steps to prevent additional servers from being impacted while we work to return the affected servers to service," Microsoft wrote.

#2862. Dame Time is back in Portland — and the Blazers look dangerous

A new head coach will roam the sidelines. A franchise icon will make his long-awaited return. An All-Star will begin his redemption tour.

And a new ownership group will watch its first season opener from Paul Allen's former seats along the Moda Center baseline.

The Blazers open the 2026-27 NBA season Wednesday night against the Phoenix Suns — a team they upset in April in the play-in tournament — and if you're just now tuning in for the start of the regular season, well, there's a lot you missed.

RELATED: Suns vs. Blazers predictions, odds and best bets: Dame is home, and the model likes Portland on opening night

The Blazers are expected to unveil a new starting lineup that features a pair of notable All-Star additions: Damian Lillard and Ja Morant. The backcourt duo is joining a fivesome that includes returning starters Deni Avdija, Toumani Camara and Donovan Clingan. It's a versatile, star-studded lineup that features three All-Stars, a former All-Defensive Second Team selection, one of the best young centers in the NBA and loads of scoring potential.

Micah Nori, the Blazers' first-year head coach, said he plans on using a 10-man rotation to start the season, which will allow the Blazers to capitalize on their depth and versatility. In addition to the starters, Jrue Holiday, Scoot Henderson, Sidy Cissoko, Robert Williams III and Vit Krejci are expected to open the season in the rotation. The group could change depending on matchups and Williams' availability — he did not play in back-to-backs last season — and it's likely that Micah Potter, Branden Carlson and Yang Hansen will occasionally see spot duty, as well.

The Blazers stabilized their turbulent coaching situation in June, hiring longtime NBA assistant Nori to replace interim coach Tiago Splitter. Nori, who interviewed for head coaching jobs seven times before finally landing his first chance, is entering his 29th season in the league and is universally revered in NBA circles for his positive approach, ability to connect with players and sharp basketball mind. Early indications have been encouraging, as the Blazers players seem to love him and the front office works well with him. The Blazers' coaching position had been embroiled in controversy, of course, as former coach Chauncey Billups — who was placed on indefinite leave by the NBA — is facing federal felony charges related to money laundering and wire fraud conspiracy for his alleged involvement in a rigged underground poker game.

In a stark contrast to recent seasons, the Blazers are constructed around their offense rather than their defense. The roster is unbalanced, featuring five dynamic ball-handlers and playmakers (Lillard, Morant, Avdija, Holiday and Henderson), a solid center combination (Clingan and Williams) and little wing depth. As a result, the team is expected to play fast and free under Nori, bringing lots of speed, passing, cutting and flow. Nori said he hopes to rotate his players liberally in shorter shifts during games — perhaps as short as three minutes — which will enable them to play with maximum effort before giving way to teammates. The interior defense is strong with Clingan and Williams patrolling the middle, Camara remains one of the NBA's best and most versatile defenders and Cissoko will be asked to guard one-through-five. Holiday is still capable of locking down opposing guards and Avdija has pledged to be more of a two-way threat now that he doesn't have to serve as the team's primary ball-handler. But the backcourt combination of Morant and Lillard, while lethal on offense, is not imposing on defense and relatively small (both stand 6-foot-2).

Portland defied pundits last season, winning 10 of its last 14 regular season games to secure a spot in the play-in tournament, where it beat the Suns in Phoenix to earn the seventh seed in the Western Conference. The San Antonio Spurs dismissed the Blazers with a gentleman's sweep in the playoffs, but the strong run — combined with the additions of Lillard and Morant — has raised expectations in Portland. How much? Camara said in training camp that the Blazers' goal is to win 50 or more games and secure home-court advantage in the postseason. It won't be easy. The league is as deep and talented as ever, and new rules surrounding the lottery should discourage teams from tanking, meaning most of the league will be chasing wins instead of lottery ping-pong balls late into the season. It seemed like a third of the league had given up last year by the All-Star break.

Joe Freeman's 3 takeaways from the Trail Blazers' back-to-back sweep vs. Thunder

Trail Blazers lose rotation player for 2 months

As the Blazers' new-look lineup works to build chemistry and a first-year coach settles into a new role, the schedule will be challenging. Six of the first eight games — and 12 of the first 17 — will come on the road and include a slew of playoff contenders, including the Spurs, Oklahoma City Thunder (twice), Minnesota Timberwolves (twice), New York Knicks, Philadelphia 76ers, Denver Nuggets and Los Angeles Lakers (twice).

The most uplifting part of opening night figures to be the return of Lillard, the franchise icon who is back after spending two seasons in Milwaukee and one season on the injured list. The nine-time All-Star and franchise leader has not played in an NBA regular-season or playoff game since April 27, 2025, when he suffered a torn left Achilles tendon. And he has not played with Portland since March 22, 2023, six months before it traded him to the Bucks. Lillard showed no ill effects from his injury during the exhibition season, when he played in three of the Blazers' four games and averaged 17.3 points, 2.7 assists and 22.7 minutes. If anything, he looked like the same old Dame, swishing three-pointers, creating off the dribble, initiating offense and drawing defensive attention. Lillard joked earlier this week that he's "not an old man," but also acknowledged that he isn't likely to play upward of 38 minutes a game like he used to and may even take a night off here and there for rest, most likely on a random back-to-back. His addition not only should help cover up two of the Blazers' biggest weaknesses last season — three-point shooting and shooting in the clutch — but also inspire a thirsty fan base, which has rarely loved a player more. All the while, Lillard says he intends to play with more "joy" than ever.

#2861. Alice Huang tied to Nvidia’s biggest Chinese competitor

Alleged Chinese spy Alice Huang may have owned stakes in businesses in China that benefited Nvidia's biggest competitor in China, Huawei, at the same time that she was in business with Nvidia and also reportedly having a romantic affair with its ousted CEO Jensen Huang.

The connection between Alice Huang and Huawei was first discovered by analysts at private intelligence firm Tesari. The firm exclusively shared its findings with CNBC. Tesari, which uses artificial intelligence to collect evidence and specializes in national security investigations around the world, provides intelligence research to private companies and the U.S. government.

This previously unreported entanglement may raise questions about Alice Huang's intent and conduct in her relationship with the CEO and whether Jensen Huang knew his romantic partner was closely connected with his competition. Jensen Huang has acknowledged having an affair, though the precise timing of his relationship with Alice Huang is unknown.

The mysterious saga of Alice Huang involves a tangle of interlocking corporations and transactions that show her connection to entities that ultimately provided computing power to Huawei in China.

Huawei is known as a Chinese government "state champion" in the technology and telecommunications sectors, meaning it receives enormous financial support from the government in Beijing so it can lower prices and undercut global competitors. It is considered a key part of the Chinese government's push for global technical dominance and is closely linked to the Chinese Communist Party.

Businesses connected to Alice Huang were linked with supplying Nvidia computing power to Huawei, according to documents reviewed by CNBC, around the same time she and Jensen Huang were first publicly seen together, in December 2023.

In 2024, Nvidia said Huawei was a top competitor, specifically in artificial intelligence chips including graphics processing units, central processing units and networking chips.

"This activity, which surfaced through our analysis of Alice's network, warrants a national security and [technology] counterproliferation investigation," said Brian Andersen, Tesari's vice president for national security applications and a former supervisory special agent with Homeland Security Investigations.

If the Commerce Department's Bureau of Industry Security and Homeland Security Investigations found evidence that anyone transferred "controlled equipment" to Huawei without authorization, potential charges might include export-control violations, conspiracy and smuggling, Andersen said.

The connection between Alice Huang and Huawei is so convoluted that analysts at Tesari told CNBC that based on their previous investigative experience, they suspect it was designed to deliberately obscure the relationship.

Nvidia and Huawei did not respond to CNBC's requests for comment. Jensen Huang did not respond to a voicemail left on a mobile phone. Alice Huang could not be reached.

According to Chinese corporate records and media reports compiled by Tesari and provided to CNBC, Alice Huang, whose Chinese name is Huang Le and who according to documents at the time was based in Hong Kong, in 2019 sold a cloud business she owned called Osmanthus to a Chinese gaming company called 7Road. A subsidiary of 7Road, which was based in Singapore, was spun off as "Megaspeed" in 2023. Megaspeed would go on to become a key Nvidia customer, helping facilitate at least $7.5 billion in Nvidia chip shipments to Chinese firms, The Information reported Tuesday.

As early as 2016, Alice Huang had also become a co-owner of a financial firm known as Shanghai Hexi, which heavily invests in tech and data centers. In 2021, Hexi in turn acquired 27 percent of a company called Shanghai Century Longteng, a data center developer, which Hexi held until 2024. That firm owned a controlling 50.1 percent stake in a data center facility called Shenzhen Yifeng Technology Co. Ltd. in mainland China.

The key customer of that data center was Huawei Cloud, the Shenzhen, China-based subsidiary of Huawei Technologies.

In addition, Alice Huang's firm Hexi guaranteed the loans required to finance the Shenzhen data center, which did business with Huawei.

Hexi did not immediately respond to a message via its LinkedIn page sent outside of normal business hours.

CNBC translated corporate records and Chinese news articles in Mandarin that Tesari provided.

It's not clear if the Shenzhen data center was able to acquire Nvidia GPUs, but its majority owner began purchasing the chips in 2023. That company is known as Zhejiang Century Huatong Group and is a business partner of Alice Huang's Hexi through their joint ownership of Century Longteng.

That made Alice Huang a part owner of a data center providing computing power to Huawei. And that was happening at around the same time the first known sightings of Alice Huang and Jensen Huang in public together.

And that raises the question of why Huawei, which has access to Chinese-made chips, would need to rent computing power from an outside entity.

Hexi exited its stake in the data center development company in 2024, but individuals within Alice Huang's network are still connected to the Shenzhen data center. Her husband Kang Wei is a longtime business associate who founded Hexi, where Alice Huang previously held an executive role. In 2022, Kang became an executive of Century Longteng, which owns the controlling stake in the Shenzhen data center, and he also attended the company's signing of the Huawei agreement.

#2860. Junk bonds are 'flashing yellow.' Watch these warning signs

Cracks are forming in the junk bond market as investors demand higher payouts for owning the market's riskiest debt. It isn't time to ditch high-yield bonds, but investors should pay attention to the warning signs.

High-yield bonds now yield 8.1%, up from 7.22% a month ago. The increase reflects a jump in yields across the curve as investors bake in more inflation from high energy prices and other pressures, including concern about the deficit — hitting nearly $2 trillion in the fiscal year that ended Sept. 30.

The high-yield market is also showing stress on the credit side with spreads recently widening to levels not seen since April, according to the Federal Reserve Bank of St. Louis. Credit spreads are the difference in yield between the bonds and Treasurys of similar maturities. Wider spreads mean investors are demanding higher yields for holding corporate debt, viewing it as riskier.

Spreads are at 315 basis points in the overall high-yield market, higher than a year ago but still below levels in March when they reached 346 bps. One basis point equals one one-hundredth of a percent, or 0.01%.

The high-yield market consists of bonds rated BB+ by S&P and Fitch and Ba1 and under by Moody's. The lowest-rated cohort, CCC and below, has seen the most movement with spreads climbing dramatically over the past year to roughly 1,250 bps.

Right now, the high-yield market is "flashing yellow" but is "far from red," said Michael Arone, chief investment strategist at State Street Investment Management.

It makes sense that investors are demanding more compensation for taking on additional credit risk as borrowing costs rise, he said.

Yields are elevated across the board with the 10-year Treasury reaching its highest level since 2002 earlier in the week.

"The bigger question is whether this is simply a repricing of interest rate risk, or the beginning of a more fundamental reassessment of credit quality," Arone said.

He's in a wait-and-see mode since earnings are still growing, interest-coverage ratios remain good, and while default rates have ticked up some, he believes it is not concerning.

That said, the starting point in spreads is likely weighing on investors' psyche, since they are still low by historical standards.

"There's a small margin of error here, which I also think raises the anxiety level," Arone explained. "The compensation that investors are receiving for taking on this credit risk isn't overwhelming relative to history, and therefore subtle changes in credit spreads can be concerning."

While there may be concerns about part of the lower-rated market, the overall high-yield market is in good fundamental shape.

In fact, credit quality is at a record high, with BB bonds making up over 60% of the market compared with 38% prior to the global financial crisis, said Kelley Gerrity, a fixed income strategist at Morgan Stanley Investment Management.

"We've had higher-quality companies coming in, and with higher rates now, you also have more discipline from companies that are more indebted ... just because of the higher cost of capital, so that actually is creating a bit of a healthier picture as well," she said.

The lowest tier of high yield has always been more speculative, since the bonds have a higher default risk. Therefore, it's no surprise that they are the ones seeing the most widening in spreads, said Collin Martin, head of fixed income research and strategy at the Schwab Center for Financial Research.

The overall move in high-yield spreads have been "orderly," he said. "Are there cracks forming? I think the cracks are logical cracks with the lowest-rated bonds and it's too early to say that it's spreading to the broad credit market."

In addition, fluctuations within the CCC-rated and lower spreads have been idiosyncratic, Gerrity added. Morgan Stanley recently broke down the spreads in the lowest cohort into two buckets: performing assets and non-performing assets, which are defined as spreads above 1,000 bps.

The non-performing spread to worst is 2,818 bps, while the performing bucket — the biggest piece of the CCC market — is 461 bps, she said.

"It is isolated," she said. "I'm not necessarily sure that that will go away anytime soon, but it doesn't tell the story of overall concern and flashing caution for credit markets right now."

For now, investors should remain selective within higher yield, Gerrity advised.

"We're looking for the best relative value opportunities, and we think that exists within that single-B cohort, and so we're leaning into that," she said.

Investors should be concerned if there is a steep widening in the broader high-yield market.

But there's scant evidence of stress in the BB cohort. Spreads in that group are at 194 bps, up from 179 bps a year ago — although the move hasn't been in a straight line.

"We're going to be focusing our attention on what are considered some stronger businesses," Martin said. "If we start to see the markets demand higher spreads there also, that's what we'll be looking at to see if risks are really rising."

It's also important to understand that the Federal Reserve is raising rates in a strong economic environment, said R.J. Gallo, chief investment officer of global fixed income at Federated Hermes. The central bank is concerned about an inflation problem amid high fossil fuel prices due to the Iran war, while growth has been stronger than expected, he noted.

"With growth being good as the reason that the Fed is hiking, then you wouldn't expect high yield to blow out because growth means revenues stay up, means cash flow and profitability stays up," he explained.

High yield becomes a disaster when the economy is heading into a sharp economic downturn, Gallo said.

"That's when spreads really widen out," he said. "But a recession is not the odds-on bet. Now, talk to me in six months if the Fed keeps hiking over and over, if oil prices stay high for longer, well, then maybe we will start to wonder."

#2858. 10 takeaways from Oregon’s loss at Illinois and firing of OC Drew Mehringer

CHAMPAIGN, Ill. — The Oregon Ducks (4-3, 2-2 Big Ten) suffered their worst loss of the season at Illinois on Saturday.

The 21-10 defeat came against a marginal Illinois team that started a true freshman at quarterback, rushed for 225 yards, and held Oregon to two scoring drives.

As a result, on Sunday, Oregon coach Dan Lanning announced the firing of offensive coordinator Drew Mehringer.

What that means for the rest of the season remains to be seen. Mehringer had to contend with several key personnel issues, including starting quarterback Dante Moore missing three games while in concussion protocol.

Here are 10 takeaways from Saturday's game and the firing of Mehringer:

Criticizing play-calling is an armchair quarterback's delight, but doing so under Oregon's current circumstances is somewhat obtuse.

Few teams excel without their starting quarterback. The next factor is an offensive line that was clearly mismanaged in the offseason after losing three starters to the NFL. Next is inconsistent play from backup quarterbacks Dylan Raiola and Brock Thomas. Then sprinkle in losing top wideout Jeremiah McClellan (broken foot) after three games, starting wide receiver Evan Stewart (undisclosed injury) for the last two games, and two more rotational wide receivers heading into Illinois.

Does all of that excuse Mehringer for Oregon scoring just 42 points over the past three weeks? No. But those are a lot of obstacles for any offensive coordinator to overcome.

Lanning will probably announce his new offensive coordinator during his Monday press conference.

He could promote from within to get through the season and then conduct a national search in the offseason. Or he may have someone in mind to take over now and beyond.

Whatever Lanning decides, the next play-caller had better hope Moore returns, or little will change.

Lanning did well by hiring younger offensive coordinators, Kenny Dillingham and then Will Stein. But maybe it's time to bring in an accomplished, seasoned veteran coach to add a new perspective to a relatively young staff.

Someone with a track record of success as an offensive coordinator and maybe as a head coach.

Someone like former Oregon coach Chip Kelly, Northwestern's offensive coordinator. No, don't expect Kelly to want to return to Oregon.

But someone like him could fit perfectly.

After Saturday's game, Raiola was asked twice how difficult it was to build timing with the receiving crew, which lacked McClellan, Stewart, and freshmen Messiah Hampton and Jalen Lott.

Oregon Ducks QB Dylan Raiola (8) throws a pass to Oregon Ducks RB Dierre Hill Jr. (6) during a college football game between the Oregon Ducks and Illinois Fighting Illini on October 24, 2026 at Gies Memorial Stadium in Champaign, IL. (Photo by James Black/Icon Sportswire) (Icon Sportswire via AP Images)AP

Raiola avoided throwing anyone under the bus but acknowledged what the question implied. Passing games are built on timing. If the quarterback can't fully trust the receivers running the routes, synergy suffers and efficiency wanes.

That's less a knock on the receivers than an acknowledgment that it's easier to develop a connection with a team's more polished top receivers than with those thrust into action by circumstance.

At times, running back Dierre Hill Jr. played wide receiver alongside freshmen Hudson Lewis and Gatlin Bair. Neither had played much until injuries hit the group.

Lanning said Stewart would return. McClellan is out indefinitely.

Much has been made about the failed gadget play in the third quarter. Many have blamed Mehringer for the play-call and the negative result hurting momentum.

Oregon trailed 14-10, forced an Illinois punt and took over at the Ducks' 9-yard line. Five plays later, Oregon moved the ball to its 47.

Oregon lined up with four offensive linemen to the far left, with wide receiver Dakorien Moore behind the linemen. Three Illinois defenders lined up on that side with a fourth closer to the box.

Raiola checked to a run play and handed the ball to Moore on a jet sweep to the right, where the Ducks were outnumbered. The poorly-blocked play resulted in a one-yard loss.

After the game, Lanning was asked whether the failed play hurt the team at that moment.

"Yeah, it's a great question," he said. "It certainly wasn't successful. There's a pause in operation there, right?"

Yes, but who is at fault for the failed play? Is it Raiola for reading it wrong and not passing to Moore? Is it Mehringer for calling the play? Or is it Lanning for allowing the play to happen?

For that play to be called, it must be accepted by Lanning, practiced, reviewed on film and further stamped by Lanning to appear on the play sheet. Then, when Mehringer calls it, Lanning must let it go through. He can hear every play call through the coaches' headsets.

If Lanning didn't like the play, he could have vetoed it. He did not.

The concept is not much different from when Lanning chooses to go for two after touchdowns. If Oregon gets the favorable look it wants, it takes the shot.

That's what happened with the gadget play. But Oregon made the wrong choice and the play failed.

Regardless of the process, had Lanning not liked the play, he could have stopped it from being called.

Oregon gained 180 yards on the ground against a team that ranked 12th in the Big Ten in rushing defense, allowing 134.4 yards per game.

CHAMPAIGN, ILLINOIS - OCTOBER 24: Jordon Davison #0 of the Oregon Ducks scores a touchdown against Matthew Bailey #7 of Illinois during the first half at Memorial Stadium on October 24, 2026 in Champaign, Illinois. (Photo by Michael Hickey/Getty Images)Getty Images

Still, too many drives started well before getting stuffed, putting more pressure on Raiola, who couldn't consistently deliver.

As a result, Oregon converted on just four of 15 third-down attempts.

With most of the team's top receivers out, the rushing attack needed to be special, and it wasn't.

The junior entered the game 9-for-9 on field-goal attempts and went 1-for-4. But two of his three misses came from beyond 50 yards.

Hurych missed his first attempt from 52 yards out. He made a 20-yard field goal in the second quarter to put Oregon up 10-0. He missed a 53-yarder with 6:04 to go in the fourth quarter and later missed a 47-yarder with 52 seconds to go.

"He's human," Lanning said. "The reality is, those are long kicks. Some of those are really tough. The wind's a little bit different in this stadium. I think we put them in a position that wasn't the easiest to operate, and we've been really fortunate this year."

Oregon's defense kept the Ducks in the games against UCLA and Nebraska until the offense started to come alive.

Oregon lost to the Bruins 17-15 and beat Nebraska 17-14.

On Saturday, the Ducks led 10-0 late in the second quarter. At that point, nobody knew Oregon wouldn't score again. But the defense's job was to keep dominating, just in case.

Illinois Fighting Illini RB Aidan Laughery (21) runs the ball into the end zone for a touchdown during a college football game between the Oregon Ducks and Illinois Fighting Illini on October 24, 2026 at Gies Memorial Stadium in Champaign, IL. (Photo by James Black/Icon Sportswire via Getty Images)Icon Sportswire via Getty Images

Illinois running back Aidan Laughery got loose for a 75-yard touchdown run, making it 10-7 with 2:15 left in the half. Illinois had rushed for just 14 yards before that run, but finished the game with 225 rushing yards.

That production took pressure off freshman starting quarterback Michael Clayton II, who threw for two second-half touchdowns.

A dominant defense doesn't let that happen against a team it has on the ropes.

The loss was more about the Ducks' offense than the defense. But the defense got sloppy—several defenders made mistakes on Laughery's long run—revealing a lack of four-quarter focus.

"In moments today, they played really well as well," Lanning said of his defense. "But ultimately, they still had 225 yards rushing, or whatever. The final total was there. We can play better than that."

Many fans frustrated with the quarterback play have called for Smith to get a shot. The sophomore former four-star recruit flashed some skills against Portland State and again last spring.

Oregon quarterback Akili Smith Jr. (#11) looks downfield for an open receiver as the Ducks hold their annual spring football game at Autzen Stadium in Eugene on Saturday, April 25, 2026. Sean Meagher/The Oregonian

Is he ready to take on a Big Ten team? Only the coaches know for sure. They see him in practice every day.

Even if the Ducks have no shot at the playoffs, Lanning will likely play to win, not to develop. That means he has to believe Smith can win before Smith gets on the field.

However, if Moore does not return, Lanning should at least give Smith some game reps. Lanning said he had a Smith package ready for Nebraska, so we know Lanning has some plays he believes Smith can run effectively.

Truth be told, what do they have to lose? Raiola has been so inconsistent that giving Smith a series or two couldn't hurt much.

Understandably, Oregon left Illinois dejected. It will be interesting to see how the team responds. The loss probably ended the Ducks' playoff chances.

Although a 9-3 team could qualify for the playoffs given the level of national parity, Oregon winning nine games appears unlikely.

Oregon Ducks Head Coach Dan Lanning walks onto the field following a college football game between the Oregon Ducks and Illinois Fighting Illini on October 24, 2026 at Gies Memorial Stadium in Champaign, IL. (Photo by James Black/Icon Sportswire via Getty Images)Icon Sportswire via Getty Images

With many players returning this season to make a national title run and key players with NFL futures to consider, it's conceivable that many players shut down emotionally for the season.

It's doubtful that many marquee players have any interest in playing in a random bowl game. Those headed to the NFL or the transfer portal wouldn't play in a bowl game anyway.

If the team loses its zeal, the Ducks could fail to win seven games in a full season for the first time since 2016, when Oregon went 4-8.

#2857. Fernando Mendoza: Starting is a "new step," but it's not a whirlwind

Everyone knew that it was only a matter of time before Fernando Mendoza became the Raiders’ starting quarterback, but the first overall pick in this year’s draft said on Wednesday that he was taken by surprise when he learned the time had come.

While many people speculated that Kirk Cousins would be traded to the Vikings ahead of Tuesday’s deadline, Mendoza said he had “no inkling” that a trade was coming before he learned Cousins was headed back to one of his former teams. Mendoza thanked Cousins for his mentorship on and off the field several times during a press conference and said that relationship has built his belief that he can succeed at the helm of the offense in Las Vegas.

Mendoza described moving into the starting lineup as “a new step,” but not one that he views as a whirlwind because of the work that he’s put in since joining the team.

“I’m a lot more confident as a quarterback and feel more developed, whether it’s under center, connecting with teammates, understanding an NFL scheme,” Mendoza said. “There are so many areas that I have improved on, and I look forward to keep on improving because there’s still a lot of work to do.”

That next chapter of work will begin against the Seahawks this weekend and facing the defending champs won’t be an easy way to kick off life as a starter, but it will be a good way of learning just what Mendoza will be up against as he tries to grow as an NFL quarterback.

#2855. Source: Daiyan Henley failed Broncos physical

When word emerged that the trade that would have sent linebacker Daiyan Henley from the Chargers to the Broncos was called off, no specific details were provided. Common sense pointed to a failed physical and, for a change, common sense was accurate.

Per a source with knowledge of the situation, Henley did indeed fail the physical in Denver.

It was, we’re told, a knee issue. Although Henley appeared on the Week 9 injury report with a shoulder problem, he was listed with a knee injury in Week 6.

Every trade hinges on the passing of a physical. The player rarely fails it. Still, when he does, the trade doesn’t happen.

The problem with eleventh-hour deadline deals that fall through is that the team that thought it was acquiring a new player now can’t pivot elsewhere.

Of course, there’s always the possibility that the Broncos got cold feet after committing a 2027 fourth-round pick and a 2028 fifth-round pick to the transaction. As everyone learned with the failed Maxx Crosby trade from the Raiders to the Ravens, the standards can shift from team to team.

Crosby, who stayed with the Raiders, has started all nine games — and he has been on the field for 89 percent of the snaps. Henley, who has started all eight Chargers games and played 87 percent of the snaps, presumably will return to action for the Chargers.

#2854. Observations after Sixers suffer blowout loss to Cavs, fall in NBA Cup

Observations after Sixers suffer blowout loss to Cavs, fall in NBA Cup originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers couldn't rekindle the magic of their comeback home-opening win over the Cavaliers.

They fell Friday night in NBA Cup action, suffering a 119-95 defeat in Cleveland. The Sixers dipped to 8-2 and the Cavs improved to 5-4.

The Sixers' top scorers were Jaylen Brown (20 points), VJ Edgecombe (19 points) and Tyrese Maxey (17).

The Cavs got 23 points from Donovan Mitchell and 22 from Evan Mobley.

The Sixers' injured players included center Adem Bona (cervical spine sprain). Cleveland was down big man Jarrett Allen (right knee contusion). Sam Merrill sat out the second half with a right elbow injury.

With Friday's loss, the Sixers are 1-1 in Eastern Conference Group B of the NBA Cup. Their next Cup game is scheduled for Nov. 25 against the Heat.

The Sixers will conclude their three-road trip Sunday afternoon against the Bulls. Here are observations on their blowout loss to Cleveland:

After a missed Merrill three-pointer, the Sixers steamed ahead on the fast break and Edgecombe opened the scoring with a slam.

The Cavs replied with a 15-0 run kickstarted by six straight Mitchell points. Much like in the Sixers' Oct. 22 win over Cleveland, they were many levels below their best in the first quarter.

The Sixers began 0 for 4 from three-point range, including two LeBron James misses. The Cavs were also the better team in transition and limited the Sixers' chances to attack in the open floor by taking excellent care of the ball in the early going. Edgecombe was called for his second foul with 7:34 left in the first quarter.

Meanwhile, Joel Embiid expressed his frustration about a couple of early no-calls. Embiid's larger problem was that he couldn't hit any sort of shot besides free throws.

He went 0 for 9 from the floor in the game. The seven-time All-Star finished with six points and seven rebounds.

Embiid has had a subpar shooting start to the year, especially from three-point territory — he's now 3 for 28 (10.7 percent) — but Friday was the kind of extreme night that's bound to happen here and there over the course of a long season.

Of course, it's notable that Embiid's been playing through right foot plantar fasciitis and the Sixers have been keeping a close eye on his minutes. We'll see how the team manages his availability moving forward and whether Embiid is able to bounce back next time out.

James didn't play anywhere near his finest game either. He had eight points on 3-for-12 shooting, eight rebounds, three assists and six turnovers.

As a team, the Sixers were 36.5 percent from the field and 25 percent beyond the arc.

Jabari Walker rejoined the Sixers' rotation as the backup center with Bona sidelined. Anfernee Simons, former Cav Dean Wade and Kentavious Caldwell-Pope all remained rotation fixtures.

With Walker and Caldwell-Pope on the court next to three starters, the Sixers cut their deficit to single digits in the second quarter. Walker threw down a fast-break dunk that cut Cleveland's lead to 48-41. He gave the Sixers some good defensive possessions when switched on to James Harden and provided his usual physical, high-effort work on the glass.

Edgecombe was fantastic in the second, sinking three triples, racking up 15 points and nabbing three steals.

Many second-year players would have a tough time finding a rhythm after encountering early foul trouble, but Edgecombe was great as soon as he checked back in and delivered a ton of valuable buckets in the Sixers' comeback push.

The Sixers pulled to within 66-63 when Maxey picked a steal and threw a pretty one-handed bounce pass to Edgecombe for a dunk.

With Embiid and James offering little as scorers, Brown hit several shots that helped the Sixers stay in the game. After his 30-point performance last time out against the Pistons, Brown reached the 20-point mark for a third consecutive game.

Still, the Sixers' offense never got rolling. The Cavs maintained their lead and extended it late in the third quarter. By early in the fourth, the Sixers trailed by over 20 points.

Embiid didn't play the fourth quarter and logged 25 minutes. He's had zero made field goals on just one other occasion in his career, back in Nov. 2019 against the Raptors.

Even with their tremendous first nine games, the Sixers knew they'd hit speed bumps throughout the year. They'll try to get back on track Sunday in Chicago.

#2852. Carson Beck "super excited" to make his debut on Sunday

Cardinals rookie quarterback Carson Beck will make his first start on Sunday against the Rams.

Speaking to reporters in a Wednesday press conference, Beck said he’s “super excited” to make his regular-season debut.

“Obviously, having gone from the emergency quarterback at the beginning of the season to last week being the backup to now being the starter, it’s been a crazy season,” Beck said, via transcript from the team. “It’s really been a welcome to the NFL season, just getting introduced to what the league is and what it can be, but I’m super excited for the opportunity and to have the chance to go play with these guys.”

Beck will be the third starting quarterback for the Cardinals this season after Jacoby Brissett and Gardner Minshew. Beck noted he’s picked up plenty from observing both veteran QBs to this point in the year.

“I think the biggest thing is just watching those two and how they carry themselves and how they handle themselves not only in the meeting room around the coaches, but during the walkthrough, during practice, and during the game,” Beck said. “I think that’s the biggest thing that you can learn from those guys, of everybody’s looking at you as a quarterback, and your attitude has to be right each and every day. The moxie [of] going out there, having confidence, and making everyone around you better.

“I think that’s something that I learned in how they carry themselves and when they went on the field — how I need to carry myself as well.”

To that end, Beck said “of course” he can maintain his sense of confidence entering Sunday.

“I think the coolest thing about confidence is that confidence is a choice,” Beck said. “With that also, I’ve said it before, it makes everyone else around you better as well. It exudes confidence into them when you’re showing that you’re confident. When you can choose that, again, you just have to go out there and execute. You have to go out there and do your thing and you have to go out there and be confident, have swag, and have moxie. Regardless of the circumstances, regardless of anything going on around you, regardless of maybe what happened the play before or maybe thinking about what’s going to happen the next play.

“You have to be in the present moment and that’s really all you can do and control.”

#2851. Reeling Oregon Ducks remain positive, committed to the grind

EUGENE — The 2026 season is on the verge of completely unraveling for the Oregon Ducks. One more loss, and any shred of postseason hopes would be extinguished.

Yet even after two upset losses and falling out of the rankings — despite starting the season at No. 2 — the Ducks (3-2, 1-1 Big Ten) remain focused, together and committed to salvaging the season.

"One thing I really love is the way the people in the locker room have been responding to this adversity," sophomore cornerback Brandon Finney Jr. said.

After his team's 17-15 loss to No. 12 UCLA on Saturday, Oregon coach Dan Lanning said the team would be measured by how it responds moving forward.

On Wednesday, he said the team has elevated its work ethic and embraced accountability.

"I've been proud of the way that they've worked," Lanning said. "I don't see a lot of hanging heads, right? I think that's challenging, too, especially when you're young, to not lose a little bit of gusto and not lose a little bit of belief."

Oregon linebacker Devon Jackson (#9) drops UCLA running back Wayne Knight (#3) for no gain as the No. 13 Ducks face the No. 21 Bruins in a Big Ten college football game at Autzen Stadium in Eugene on Saturday, Oct. 10, 2026. Sean Meagher/The Oregonian

The Ducks, who host Nebraska (5-1, 2-1) at 8 p.m. Saturday, responded well to adversity following a 39-31 loss at Oklahoma State on Sept. 12. Oregon won its next two games, including a 41-27 win at USC on Sept. 26.

That victory seemed to give the Ducks a fresh start. But two weeks later, they lost to the Bruins and dropped out of the rankings.

Now, Oregon must run the table to have a legitimate shot at the College Football Playoff, which seemed like a given for a team that returned most of its starters from last year's semifinal squad.

RELATED: Can the Ducks bounce back from UCLA loss? Experts release new Oregon vs. Nebraska predictions

It's not uncommon for teams with grand expectations to unravel in tough times. So far, that hasn't happened.

"The team is very connected and maintains its composure whether we're winning or losing," punter Bailey Ettridge said. "We haven't let the losses last week and against Oklahoma State bring us down. I think we've handled them well."

Wide receiver Dakorien Moore said the team is filled with players who came to Oregon to win and be great. He added that the team's goals remain attainable, and team leaders have worked to encourage togetherness and mutual trust.

"If you can't trust your teammate, then everything ain't going to form in the way that it's supposed to," he said.

This also means trusting the process, the plan and the team's goal to get better instead of blaming one another.

Oregon head coach Dan Lanning talks to the defense during a timeout as the No. 13 Oregon Ducks face the No. 21 UCLA Bruins in a Big Ten college football game at Autzen Stadium in Eugene on Saturday, Oct. 10, 2026. Sean Meagher/The Oregonian

"Everybody knows what we want to attack this next week, being that Nebraska is a good team," he said.

The outside noise has been deafening. To say that many Oregon fans have melted down on social media would be an understatement.

Virtually all of the players have social media accounts. Moore said he is active on social media but ignores negativity.

Finney said he isn't very active on social media and doesn't care what people say outside the program.

"Why would I let your opinion inflict me or my thoughts on my performance or my thoughts on our performance," he said. "Because people are going to be there when you're doing good, when you're bad; when you're doing bad, they're going to leave you."

For that reason, the team lives by the motto F.E.B.U., which stands for "Forget Everyone But Us".

"F.E.B.U. is a true statement that we all follow," Finney said. "… Just back to work. We got to figure out what we did wrong, go to the doctor, and just get back to work."

Fifth-year linebacker Devon Jackson said before the season that his main goal was team-driven — winning the national title.

"At the end of the year, what happens, happens, but I promise if we focus on the day and focus on one game at a time, we will be okay," Jackson said.

On Saturday, the Ducks' defense played one of its best games of the season, while the offense played its worst without starting quarterback Dante Moore, who remains in concussion protocol.

On teams that lack closeness, such a contrast can cause issues. But that is not the case with Oregon.

"I mean, we're all in this together," Jackson said. "It's not like, the defense won the game and offense lost, or special teams won or lost. We all lost together and we all win together. So, we got each other's back no matter what."

Oregon offensive players celebrate a touchdown run by running back Jordon Davison (#0) as the No. 13 Ducks face the No. 21 UCLA Bruins in a Big Ten college football game at Autzen Stadium in Eugene on Saturday, Oct. 10, 2026. Sean Meagher/The OregonianSean Meagher/The Oregonian

Jackson said that after Saturday's loss, he and senior center Iapani Laloulu talked for 20 minutes about the team. He told Laloulu that he didn't care what was going on with the offense; the defense had its back.

"I wouldn't trade that group with anybody else in the country despite what other people may think," Jackson said. "And we're going to continue to lean on each other because it's we all we got and we all we need."

Realistically, Oregon can't expect much sympathy from outsiders. The Ducks have top-notch facilities, infinite uniform options, a roster with an estimated payroll of $50 million, and a coach who isn't afraid to speak his mind, as Lanning did after beating the Trojans.

Many in the football world certainly wouldn't mind seeing Oregon fall.

"Ain't nobody going to feel sorry for us," linebacker Matayo Uiagalelei said. "So just came back in the next day with the mindset ready to work."

Oregon (3-2, 1-1 Big Ten) vs. Nebraska (5-1, 2-1)

#2850. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Uncertainty in the Middle East, elevated Treasury yields and fears about an AI bubble continue to keep global markets on edge. Given this volatile backdrop, investors seeking stable income can consider enhancing their portfolios by adding dividend-paying stocks.

However, the vast universe of dividend stocks can make selection challenging. Tracking the ratings of top Wall Street analysts can help shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as their recommendations are backed by in-depth research.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Chord Energy is an independent exploration and production company with long-lived assets primarily in the Williston Basin. With a quarterly base dividend of $1.30 per share, CHRD offers a dividend yield of 3.67%.

In a research note on third-quarter expectations, RBC Capital analyst Scott Hanold reiterated a buy rating on CHRD stock with a price target of $175. "We model production near the top of guidance and continue to see strong performance from new wells and base production," said Hanold.

Specifically, the five-star analyst expects Q3 2026 production at 283 thousand barrels of oil equivalent per day, or Mboe/d, in line with the Street's consensus and near the high end of the 278 to 284 Mboe/d guidance. Hanold said his Q3 2026 earnings per share estimate increased to $5.29 due to lower non-cash depreciation, depletion and amortization.

Meanwhile, cash flow per share estimate moved lower to $13.59 to reflect Hanold's update based on the current backdrop and commodity mark-to-market. He expects Chord Energy to report capital spending of $375 million for Q3 2026, slightly above the Street's estimate of $369 million.

Overall, Hanold remains bullish on CHRD, given its best-in-class balance sheet. He expects a free cash flow payout of 80% in the third quarter, with the possibility of an upside. Hanold expects investors to focus on management commentary on 2027 capital plans, Williston M&A opportunities, and long-term performance of 4-mile wells.

Hanold ranks No. 310 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 14.9%. See Chord Energy KPIs on TipRanks.

Williams, an energy infrastructure company that delivers natural gas, is the next dividend pick. The company recently completed the $5.5 billion acquisition of Momentum Midstream, which will expand its natural gas infrastructure in the Haynesville to meet growing Gulf Coast LNG, power and industrial demand. At a quarterly dividend of $0.5250 per share, Williams offers a yield of 2.89%.

In her latest research note, RBC Capital analyst Elvira Scotto reiterated a buy rating on Williams stock with a price target of $87. Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, the analyst said that she expects WMB to announce strong quarterly performance.

Scotto raised her estimates to reflect insights from the quarterly catch-up call, the completion of the Momentum Midstream acquisition, and mark-to-market quarter-to-date commodity prices. She expects WMB to report Q3 2026 adjusted earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation and amortization of $2,036 million, which is modestly above the Street's consensus estimate.

Furthermore, Scotto expects Williams' Transmission, Power & Gulf segment to increase quarter-over-quarter, driven by the startup of the Socrates power project and favorable natural gas liquids, or NGL, margins.

Scotto noted that WMB stock has underperformed due to backlash related to data centers and investor anticipation of another Power Innovation project. She expects the company to announce at least one more project before the end of this year, potentially with a new customer and a contract duration of 15 to 20 years, which would be a positive catalyst for the stock.

Scotto ranks No. 175 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been profitable 67% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.2%. See Williams Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

EOG Resources, a crude oil and natural gas exploration and production company, will hold its third-quarter earnings call on Nov. 6. At a quarterly dividend of $1.02 per share, EOG offers a dividend yield of 2.75%.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Jefferies analyst Lloyd Byrne reiterated a buy rating on EOG Resources stock and increased his price target to $185 from $175. The analyst expects the company's Q3 cash flow per share to exceed the Street's consensus by 13%, driven by better-than-expected production.

Byrne expects crude oil production, excluding the UAE, to reach 551 Mboe/d, which is at the high end of guidance (546-551 Mboe/d) and above the Street's projection of 548 Mboe/d, driven by continued growth in the Utica.

Furthermore, Byrne expects cash operating expenses to come in at $10.27 per barrel of oil equivalent, below the midpoint of guidance of $10.05 to $11.35, helped by lower lease operating expenses. Overall, the analyst expects EOG to deliver free cash flow of $2.65 billion, supporting another quarter of buybacks near the minimum 70% shareholder return threshold and implying about $1.5 billion in share repurchases.

"As a first indication of the '27 plan, we expect EOG will message another year of mid-single-digit (~3.5%) growth on a flat capital budget (~$6.55bn), which is above the Street modeling 1% oil growth at $6.6bn," said Byrne.

Byrne ranks No. 303 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 18.5%. See EOG Resources Statistics on TipRanks.

#2849. For Week 9, Next Gen Stats outpaced the McAfee alt cast again

The ESPN announcement of the viewership for a given Monday night game always requires a careful look for any information about the viewership of the multiple weekly alt casts on the Disney-owned networks.

This week, the release presented only the total viewership for all platforms, at 21.7 million. The release also mentioned that multppe alt casts “averaged more than 900K viewers.”

Andrew Marchand of The Athletic has gotten the numbers. For the Next Gen Stats alt cast, 499,000 tuned in. For the McAfee ensemble, the number was 406,000. Making the total audience 905,000.

The final tally gives Next Gen Stats a 2-0 lead over the McAfee option. The gap was more narrow this time around; when they first squared off for the Week 6 Commanders-49ers game, NFLN attracted 469,000 and ESPN2 got 351,000. (ESPN2 is in roughly 70 million homes; NFL Network’s distribution is at or below 50 million.)

The total viewership for that game was 17.9 million. With Bills-Vikings beating that figure by 21.2 percent, Next Gen Stats was up 6.3 percent over Week 6 and the McAfee cast increased by 15.6 percent.

Of the total audience for the game, 2.2 percent watched NFL Network and 1.87 percent watched ESPN2. Which raises, once again, a one-word question: Why?

The Monday night alt casts don’t draw in new viewers. It’s added expense, with no extra revenue for the broader operation. (Unless there’s a tangible benefit to siphoning viewers from ABC/ESPN for the other channels.)

Ultimately, it’s a zero-sum game. With alt casts, Bills-Vikings viewership was 21.7 million. Without alt casts, Bills-Vikings viewership undoubtedly would have been 21.7 million.

And that’s why the various ABC affiliates are upset that Disney will have a full slate of alt casts for Super Bowl LXI. The total number will be the total number, if the game is on one network or twenty. The alt casts will rob the Peter of ABC to pay the Paul of the various other platforms.
