#2848. Back in Philadelphia with Celtics, Paul George looks forward to ‘a regular game'

Back in Philadelphia with Celtics, Paul George looks forward to 'a regular game' originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Paul George's presence back in town was one of the many swirling morning storylines at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

For good reason, former Celtics teammates Jaylen Brown and Jayson Tatum will draw immense attention Tuesday night. After all, Brown has said there "might be a little lamb chops with some gravy" between the two, if not a full-blown beef.

But George's return to Philadelphia is more than a tiny subplot. Following two seasons in Philadelphia, he was the centerpiece of the Sixers' summer trade package to acquire Brown.

George told reporters before the Celtics' shootaround that it feels like "a regular game."

"Had I been here eight, nine, 10 years, different story," he said. "I was here two years. Obviously, we didn't have the best of luck or had a great, I think, partnership in terms of my ability to stay healthy and on the floor. So that kind of played into a lot of it.

"But obviously, it's a tremendous fan base, a great sports town to play in. So that was appreciated. There's no ill will for me. It'll be fun to just compete. And outside of that, it's a normal game."

The 36-year-old George indeed had trouble staying available for sustained stretches as a Sixer. He dealt with a variety of injuries and served a 25-game suspension last season for violating the NBA's anti-drug policy.

George did play well on both ends of the floor for much of the 2026 playoffs. He helped the Sixers come back to win a seven-game first-round series against the Celtics in historic fashion. The nine-time All-Star also brought a veteran steadiness to the Sixers' locker room and was glad to mentor younger players like VJ Edgecombe.

"Just his routine … even when he got hot, he didn't try to force it," Jabari Walker said Tuesday morning. "His game was smooth and I think that's how he is as a person, just not forcing anything and having a calm energy."

George has certainly enjoyed his early days as a Celtic.

Through nine games, he's averaged 20.4 points, 5.2 rebounds, 4.1 assists and 2.3 steals while shooting 42.5 percent from three-point range. Boston will enter Tuesday night's matchup second in the Eastern Conference standings at 8-2, the Sixers first at 9-2.

"I've been kind of blown away," George said. "I think it exceeded my expectations since being in Boston. You never know how much culture is involved and how much culture changes your outlook on things and the effect it has mentally, physically, just being in an environment … and the culture that Boston presents.

"So I've been thrilled. I've been playing some of my best basketball of late, and just been enjoying grinding it out with these guys out here. It's a fun style of play and I can't talk (enough) about just the thrill, the happiness and the excitement and joy that it has brought being here."

He was asked what sort of reception he expects from Sixers fans.

"Not sure," George said. "In some ways, I helped land them a future piece for them. So with that, not sure how the reception would be."

#2847. Oregon QB Dante Moore ruled out for rest of 2026 season

Oregon Ducks quarterback Dante Moore will miss the rest of the season, coach Dan Lanning announced Monday night.

"I'm not anticipating him being back this season," Lanning said.

"Really got clarity for us this past week, for him," Lanning added. "And I think if it were up to Dante in any shape, way, he would want to be out there playing for the guys. But, unfortunately, it's not really up to Dante. It's not really up to me. We can control the setting and practice where he's able to do things for us, but that's not always the case in the game."

Lanning said he expects Moore, who has run scout-team offense in practice, to fully recover.

"That hasn't changed," Lanning said. "Definitely feel that way, but for him to be out there on the field for us right now, that's not gonna happen."

Lanning added that Moore would remain involved with the team.

"He's still going to be a big part of this team and helping us move forward and find opportunities for us to be better," Lanning said.

The announcement comes days after the reeling Ducks lost 21-10 at Illinois on Saturday in the third straight game Moore missed while in concussion protocol.

Before the Illinois game, Moore told The Oregonian/OregonLive that he felt good but didn't know if he'd return this season and was taking it "one day at a time."

He then began throwing passes on the field in shorts and a sweatshirt.

Saturday's loss all but knocked Oregon (4-3, 2-2 Big Ten) out of the national playoff picture and led Lanning to fire offensive coordinator Drew Mehringer on Sunday.

Without Moore, Oregon scored 42 points over three games and lost twice while rotating between backup quarterbacks Dylan Raiola and Brock Thomas.

Moore was injured on Sept. 26 at USC while sliding to end a 10-yard run to the USC 13-yard line. As Moore began to slide, Trojans linebacker Desman Stephens II hit him in the head with his shoulder.

Oregon quarterback Dante Moore, center bottom, takes a hard tackle by lSouthern California inebacker Desman Stephens II, center top, during the first half of an NCAA college football game, Saturday, Sept. 26, 2026, in Los Angeles. (AP Photo/Mark J. Terrill)AP Photo/Mark J. Terrill

Moore was taken off the field in a cart and loaded into an ambulance.

Following Moore's injury, Raiola entered the game and led the Ducks to a 41-27 win.

Moore entered the concussion protocol but returned to practice the following bye week.

Speculation was that Moore would return once he cleared the protocols. But each subsequent availability report listed Moore as out.

On Oct. 10, Oregon lost 17-15 to UCLA, with Raiola struggling through three quarters before being replaced in the fourth quarter by Thomas.

Thomas started the following week against Nebraska, but he played poorly before Raiola entered the game in the fourth quarter and led the Ducks to a 17-14 comeback win.

Oregon's realistic national playoff hopes remained alive at 4-2 — until the loss to Illinois.

At 4-3, the Ducks must win their final five games to reach 9-3 and hope enough playoff contenders finish with three losses so the Ducks can receive a berth.

If Moore enters the 2027 NFL Draft, his announcement will end his brief Ducks career, during which he led the program to one of its best seasons.

Oregon quarterback Dante Moore walks off the field after the No. 5 Ducks fall to the No. 1 Indiana Hoosiers, 56-22, in the Peach Bowl at the College Football Playoff semifinals from Mercedes-Benz Stadium in Atlanta on Friday, Jan. 9, 2025. Sean Meagher/The OregonianSean Meagher/The Oregonian

Lanning said he wasn't sure what Moore's plans were after this season.

"That's not fair for me to say or speculate," Lanning said. "Obviously, he'll have a decision again at the end of the season and what that looks like. I think we all can acknowledge that Dante is a great player and he will get an opportunity to get drafted. But what that looks like in the future, I think that's a decision for a later date. I don't know where his mind is at on that. We haven't had a conversation. That's not where my focus is at. I don't think that's where his focus is at right now. There's a reason you get time to make that decision later on."

Moore committed to Oregon in the summer of 2022. Later, he flipped to UCLA in December of 2022 rather than back up Ducks quarterback Bo Nix in 2023.

That summer, Moore learned that his mother had been diagnosed with cancer, adding to the pressure he was feeling while trying to perform as a highly touted freshman.

Moore had a rough freshman season at UCLA. His midseason struggles led Chip Kelly to bench Moore.

Following the season, Moore entered the transfer portal and committed to the Ducks in December 2023.

Moore transferred to Oregon to develop while backing up the team's new starter, Oklahoma transfer Dillon Gabriel.

That plan paid off in 2025. Moore passed for 3,565 yards, 30 touchdowns and 10 interceptions while leading the Ducks to a 13-2 record and the playoff semifinals, where they lost to eventual national champion Indiana.

Moore, a projected first-round pick in the 2026 NFL Draft, returned to Oregon for his senior season to keep improving with a team that returned eight defensive starters and was expected to contend for a national championship.

Oregon quarterback Dante Moore (#5) celebrates with running back Tradarian Ball (#4) after Ball's touchdown as the No. 21 Ducks face the Portland State Vikings in a non-conference college football game at Autzen Stadium in Eugene on Friday, Sept. 18, 2026. Sean Meagher/The Oregonian

Oregon entered the season ranked No. 2 — the highest preseason ranking in program history — and defeated Boise State, 34-27, in the opener.

Moore passed for a career-high 378 yards with three touchdown passes.

The season began to unravel the following week when the heavily favored Ducks lost 39-31 at Oklahoma State.

The following week, Oregon won 84-0 over Portland State. Next came the USC game.

The Ducks, who host No. 20 Northwestern (6-1, 3-1), could continue to start Raiola or turn to former four-star recruit Akili Smith Jr.

Chances are, Lanning will go with whichever quarterback he believes can win games.

In the offseason, Oregon is expected to seek a quarterback in the transfer portal. With high expectations at Oregon, Lanning likely won't bank on Raiola or Smith as the starter in 2027.

#2843. Longtime Steelers player, coach Dick Hoak dies at 86

The Steelers announced the death of their longtime player and coach Dick Hoak at the age of 86.

Hoak was born outside of Pittsburgh and went to Penn State before the Steelers made him a 1961 seventh-round pick. He spent 10 years as one of the team’s running backs and finished his career with 3,965 rushing yards and 1,452 receiving yards. Hoak scored 33 overall touchdowns during his time on the roster.

Hoak joined Chuck Noll’s staff as an offensive backfield coach in 1972, moved to running backs coach in 1991 and was the only one of Noll’s coaches who remained on the staff under Noll’s successor Bill Cowher. He retired in 2006 after coaching future Hall of Famers Franco Harris and Jerome Bettis and being part of five Super Bowl champions.

“We are deeply saddened by the passing of Dick Hoak, a legendary member of the Pittsburgh Steelers family,” Steelers owner Art Rooney II said in a statement. “Dick was part of the foundation of this organization and played an instrumental role in shaping the Steelers as both a player and coach during his 45 years with the team. Dick represented everything the Steelers stand for with his toughness, loyalty, dedication and an unwavering commitment to the team. First as a player, then as a coach, Dick’s contributions to this organization spanned generations.”

Hoak is a member of the Steelers’ Hall of Honor and received the Pro Football Hall of Fame’s Award of Excellence in 2025.

#2841. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on the growth potential of these 3 stocks

Fears around the artificial intelligence trade and higher pressure on oil prices are weighing on global markets, but a few stocks offer investors solid growth prospects even in choppy times.

Investors with a long-term horizon can look beyond the ongoing volatility and track the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts to pick attractive stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Health insurer UnitedHealth delivered better-than-expected results for the third quarter of 2026. The company also raised its full-year earnings outlook, reflecting continued progress on its turnaround plan.

Following the Q3 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Erin Wright reiterated a buy rating on UNH stock with a price target of $529. "UNH's beat & raise reinforces turnaround momentum, with improving MA [Medicare Advantage]/Optum Health supporting a higher '26 EPS baseline & upside to '27 consensus," said Wright.

The five-star analyst added that better-than-expected performance in the UnitedHealthcare and Optum Health businesses supported another upgrade to the company's 2026 adjusted earnings per share outlook to at least $20.50. Wright noted that UNH raised the earnings guidance despite incremental investments planned for the fourth quarter of 2026.

Furthermore, Wright highlighted management's view that the consensus 2027 EPS of $22.63 was too conservative. Reaching even the low end of the company's 13% to 16% long-term EPS growth target would imply an EPS of $23.17.

Wright raised her 2026 EPS estimate to $20.65 from $19.82 and 2027 EPS to $23.24 from $22.54, reflecting the latest upside and an improving 2026 medical loss ratio to 87.4% from 88.1%. Overall, she believes that UNH's operational performance continues to improve, increasing her confidence in its UHC strategy and Optum Health turnaround execution.

Wright ranks No. 264 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 15%. See UnitedHealth Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Social media giant Meta Platforms has been making headlines since the launch of its Muse AI agent, which is seeing solid traction. Investors look forward to updates on the company's AI strategy when it announces its third-quarter results on Oct. 21.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Guggenheim analyst Michael Morris maintained a buy rating on Meta stock and increased his price target to $850 from $700, reflecting higher estimates for 2027. He expects Muse usage momentum and engagement levels to shape investor sentiment into the Q3 earnings call.

Specifically, Morris highlighted that, according to Apptopia, Muse reached 1.3 million daily active users in five weeks versus 300,000 in the first week. Given that Muse's current adoption represents less than 0.05% of Meta's 3.6 billion daily active user base, the analyst expects management to prioritize boosting usage over near-term monetization.

"Over time, we believe Muse monetizes primarily through businesses paying to reach consumers through their agents, consistent with the economics of Meta's core advertising business, rather than consumer wallets," said the five-star analyst. He added that the checkout integrations with Shopify and PayPal reflect this framework.

While there are concerns that a rival could overtake Muse or the recently launched Muse Wonder and push Meta into a prolonged cycle of overinvestment, Morris remains bullish due to the company's strong record in scaling WhatsApp and competing strongly with TikTok via Reels as well as free distribution to an extensive user base.

Morris ranks No. 629 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Meta Platforms KPIs on TipRanks.

Next is semiconductor company Marvell Technology. Earlier this month, Marvell held its Investor Day, where it raised its total addressable market estimate to $400 billion by 2030, reflecting solid AI-led demand tailwinds.

After hosting a non-deal roadshow with Marvell's senior management following the Investor Day, Stifel analyst Tore Svanberg reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $370.

The five-star analyst said that a major focus of the roadshow was the XPU and XPU Attach mix, as Marvell's Custom business expands toward its FY31 revenue target of $30 billion. In this regard, management reiterated that it expects a balanced mix of XPU and XPU Attach revenue in FY31, though it noted that either segment could grow rapidly enough to shift the mix toward 60/40 or 40/60.

While XPU drove most of the nearly $1.5 billion Custom revenue in FY26, Svanberg expects Marvell's XPU Attach business to grow substantially through the end of the decade, leading to a more balanced mix in this business. At the Investor Day event, the company raised its FY29 Custom target from more than $10 billion to over $12 billion, mainly driven by strength in the XPU Attach business.

Svanberg said that another key topic in the roadshow was whether Marvell can secure enough capacity to meet its raised targets. Management said that it met with suppliers ahead of Investor Day to share its five-year goals and received commitments to support these targets. The analyst believes that Marvell's supply chain commitments give it a competitive edge over rivals as customers seek reliable partners for large-scale infrastructure buildouts.

Svanberg ranks No. 91 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 31.8%. See Marvell Technology Statistics on TipRanks.

#2838. Steph Curry has an oblique abdominal tear; to be reevaluated in 2 weeks

The magical 8-game winning streak came to a crashing halt last night after Steph Curry left in the first quarter with an injury and today the season at large feels on the verge of collapse as the Golden State Warriors have announced that their franchise cornerstone is out with an oblique abdominal tear.

The timeline for his return ought to be an open question at this point, as their press release mentions that he'll be reevaluated in two (2) weeks. That tracks with the reporting last night that he felt a pain he had never felt before.

This type of injury can be overcome without the need for surgery, but in that scenario, it's usually a 3-4 week timeline for recovery… if it's closer to a strain. But this is being described as a "tear," which could very easily mean a couple of months recovery time. There's a lot of fast and twisting upper body motion needed to get an open shot or disrupt a passing lane.

It was only a couple of days ago that the Warriors lost Steph's backup to a hamstring strain, so the injury picture has grown from outsized to overwhelming. Steph, Brandon Williams, Jimmy Butler, Moses Moody, Gary Payton II, Will Richard, Kristaps Porzingis — it's not as though Golden State had All-Star backups or were the deepest team in the association heading into the season, but there's "testing a team's depth" and there's "Oops! All injured," and, folks, we're here.

There's no making up for Curry's ~28 a night and clutch shots, but if there is one throughline through this weird start to a season it's that rotations have had a pretty solid flow to them in spite of the injuries. As Draymond put it last night:

"The spirit of this team is better," Green said. "We're more certain of who we are, what makes us a good team. I think that puts us in a better position to be successful without him as opposed to where we were last year. Then obviously [Yaxel Lendeborg], having a guy you can give the ball to and he can create an advantage. [Brandin Podziemski] is playing a lot better. … It puts us in a better position."

This isn't to suggest that Yaxel & Pods will each score 40 a night and everyone else who's left will step up, but this puzzle that the team had been working hard to piece together already just has to keep up that effort and see what shakes out.

Hopefully, the Warriors are able to weather this surprise storm until Steph Curry returns.

#2836. Trevor Lawrence: Trades show the confidence in our team, what we have a chance to do

The Jaguars signaled they’re all-in on the 2026 season by making several trades to fortify their roster before the deadline.

Quarterback Trevor Lawrence told reporters in his Wednesday press conference that the moves signal a positive trend for the team.

“I think you try not to look too much into it,” Lawrence said, via transcript from the team. “We have the same job to do, and I think we have a really good team that has good chemistry. Has gelled together. We like playing together, we have great camaraderie. All those things that are just hard to put your finger on sometimes. I think we have all that. I think we’ve been playing well, and so we still have a job to do, and nothing changes from that perspective.

“But I do think, obviously with the moves made, I think it shows the confidence in our team, and what we have the chance to do. I think that we’re a really good team, and now we get even better with these guys. I think that’s all it is, is just making our team even better, and we know that we’re going to have a shot. We just got to keep playing well and we got to keep winning. I think you can’t look too much into it because we still have the same job right in front of us, but I think these guys are going to help us for sure.”

Moreover, it helps that the players acquired have significant postseason experience.

“I think to get winners, to get guys that have been there before, that experience is important,” Lawrence said. “I think that is, why would you not want that if you can? Obviously, you got to look at the players you’re getting, and you want great players, but if they’ve also won Super Bowls and been to Super Bowls, I think that’s icing on the cake or whatever. That’s a plus. So, we have some guys obviously in that group that have done that. Just excited to get that experience. I think they’ve played meaningful football. They know what it looks like and going to add that value to our team.”

We’ll see how the additions start to fit in when the Jaguars play the Titans on Sunday.

#2834. Stephen Curry suffers oblique abdominal tear, will be re-evaluated in two weeks

The NBA injury news continues to pile up as the Golden State Warriors announced that Stephen Curry has suffered an oblique abdominal tear and will miss at least two weeks.

The two-time MVP and 12-time All-Star suffered the injury early in the first quarter of Wednesday night's game against the Houston Rockets with a little under five minutes elapsed in the game. Curry came off a screen and hit a three-pointer but grimaced in pain as he released the shot. He immediately signaled to the bench and, after the Warriors were able to call a timeout, Curry went into the locker room in visible pain. By the second quarter, the 38-year-old guard was already ruled out for the remainder of the game with an abdominal injury and was sent for an MRI during the second half of the game, which the Warriors lost 115-108.

After the game. Warriors head coach Steve Kerr said "there was a high level of concern" about Curry's status and relayed that Curry told him that this pain was something the 38-year-old had never felt before.

Given the level of concern the Warriors seemed to have at the end of Wednesday's game, the diagnosis could be taken as relatively good news. Curry will miss at least the next seven games, but there is a chance that's all. While the Magic's Paolo Banchero missed 34 games with an oblique injury last season, the most recent examples of NBA oblique injuries put the return-to-play timeline around 2-3 weeks. That means Curry could be back by the start of December.

While no injury to a player like Curry comes at a good time, this is a particularly tough moment for the Warriors to be forced to be without their star. Despite playing without Jimmy Butler (knee surgery), Moses Moody (knee surgery), and Kristaps Porzingis (illness), the Warriors had seemingly found their groove. They came into Wednesday night having won eight in a row. Curry was averaging 27.8 points, 3.7 rebounds, and 3.9 assists per game while shooting 50% from the field and 45.7% from beyond the arc. His elite play, along with the emergence of Yaxel Lendeborg, had made the Warriors a contender in the Western Conference, even without Butler.

It was a much-needed hot stretch after the Warriors missed the playoffs last year following a 37-45 season. Curry missed 39 games with injuries last season as well, and the Warriors were just 13-26 without him, which means they will likely have some work to do to make up ground once Curry is able to return.

In the meantime, even more will fall on the plate of Lendeborg, but Kerr will be cautious not to overload the rookie. Brandin Podziemski will be able to handle more of the scoring load, and it appears that De'Anthony Melton is the best bet to enter the starting lineup for Curry. In 25 minutes last night against the Rockets, Melton had 11 points, four rebounds, and four assists.

Given the significant injuries suffered by the Thunder, Trail Blazers, and Jazz, the Warriors, who are currently sixth in the Western Conference, remain very much in the mix. The team will just need to keep things afloat for the next two-plus weeks until their captain can return.

#2833. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Global stock markets remain volatile amid persistent geopolitical tensions and concerns about the sustainability of AI trade. Given this backdrop, investors seeking steady income can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios.

Tracking recommendations from top Wall Street analysts can help investors pick attractive stocks, as these experts assess a company's ability to pay dividends consistently.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is a dividend king. The company has increased its dividend for 39 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share (annualized dividend of $7.12 per share), CVX offers a yield of 3.14%. Chevron will announce its third-quarter results on October 30.

In an earnings preview note on U.S. oil majors, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Chevron with a price target of $241. The analyst is focused on the company's international growth prospects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa, and the Eastern Mediterranean.

Specifically, Mehta highlighted management's commentary on solid execution in Venezuela, with gross production expected to more than double to nearly 600 kbd (kilo barrels per day) by 2031 via three joint ventures. He also noted a recent oil and gas condensate discovery in offshore Angola and is awaiting commercial updates on the West Qurna 2 and Nasiriyah projects in Iraq.

Furthermore, Mehta expects CVX to report improved productivity and capital efficiency across domestic shale assets. He expects the improvement mainly in the Permian Basin, where Chevron projects advanced chemical treatments to drive about a 10% recovery uplift in new wells and bring down capital spend per barrel by about 25% year-over-year. Overall, Mehta expects Chevron to report Q3 2026 EPS of $4.95, above the consensus of $4.89.

"While unfavorable timing effects and volatile commodity prices may present quarterly earnings headwinds, we highlight healthy U.S. refining margins and strong asset utilization as partial offsets," said Mehta.

Mehta ranks No. 381 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Chevron KPIs on TipRanks.

Kinetik Holdings (KNTK) is a fully integrated, pure-play, midstream company operating in the Delaware Basin. Earlier this month, the company declared a dividend of $0.81 per share, payable on October 30, 2026. With an annualized dividend of $3.24 per share, KNTK offers a yield of 5.82%. Kinetik will announce its Q3 earnings on November 4, with a conference call scheduled the next day.

Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, Goldman Sachs analyst John Mackay reiterated a buy rating on Kinetik stock and slightly raised his price target to $58 from $57. He expects the company to report Q3 2026 EBITDA of $279 million, higher than the Street's expectation of $270 million but lower than the second quarter's EBITDA of $281 million.

The 5-star analyst expects Kinetik's Q3 performance to gain from higher gas volumes after notable curtailments in the second quarter. Mackay also expects the quarter to benefit from higher production and steady commodity margins, even though some lower-margin contracts and weaker Waha spread marketing gains could weigh on results.

Overall, Mackay increased his Q3 2026 estimates to reflect higher volumes, driven by rapid Permian egress pipeline commissioning, stronger throughput at Kings Landing I, and higher M2M commodity prices, offset by increased costs.

"As natural gas takeaway constraints ease across the Permian, investor focus centers on KNTK's YE26 volume trajectory and durability of its multi-year growth," said Mackay.

Mackay ranks No. 569 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.9%. See Kinetik's Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Moving to Crescent Energy (CRGY), an exploration and production company with activities focused in the Eagle Ford, Permian, and Uinta Basins. With a quarterly dividend of $0.12 per share (annualized dividend of $0.48 per share), CRGY's dividend yield stands at 3.56%. Earlier this month, Crescent announced an agreement to acquire Eagle Ford assets from Devon Energy (DVN).

Heading into Q3 earnings on Nov. 2, Evercore analyst Stephen Richardson reiterated a buy rating on Crescent stock and slightly lowered the price target to $17 from $18, citing dilution from new shares.

"CRGY's recent acquisition of DVN's Eagle Ford assets marks a strategic, growth-oriented move, potentially enhancing cash flow performance," said Richardson.

The 5-star analyst noted that the acquisition of the Eagle Ford assets at a net purchase price of $3.85 billion will add about 68 MBoe/d of net production and over 600 net Tier 1 locations. Richardson added that a solid track record of synergies from past deals in the Eagle Ford region increases confidence about Crescent achieving efficiencies with this deal.

Additionally, Richardson noted that the financial implications of the acquisition seem impressive, with a projected increase in cash flow per share of about 9% for 2027 to 2029.

The analyst believes that Crescent is not just expanding its business through acquisitions but positioning itself as a more durable company, regardless of market conditions.

Richardson ranks No. 478 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Crescent Energy Statistics on TipRanks.

#2832. PFT's Week 10 2026 NFL picks

I have no explanation for the last two weeks. But I’ll use an unexpected run of success to provide a warning to anyone else who wakes up one day in the midst of a hot streak.

In Week 8, I went 13-1 in the straight-up-only picks exercise. (No more picks against the spread, for reasons I’ve previously explained.) For Week 9, I ended up 14-1.

That’s 27-2 over two weeks. (Which translates to 87-48-1 for the year, which doesn’t sound nearly as good as “27-2.”)

Now for the warning, specifically to anyone who has gambled or who will be gambling on sports. The worst thing you can do is get lucky. Because you’ll think that you’ve suddenly become good.

Then, you’ll let it ride until your luck runs out. That’s when it gets dangerous. Because you’ll fool yourself into thinking that it’s just a temporary setback, that your secret formula for picking winners remains viable, and you’ll chase your losses. You’ll keep digging that hole, confident that it’s just a matter of time before your sudden genius returns.

Eventually, you’ll find yourself in an untenable financial situation.

That’s how it happens for plenty of gamblers. They don’t start out losing and keep losing. They win enough to make themselves think they know more than they do, when the reality is that no one knows anything. And then the wheels come off. And then they get desperate to put the wheels back on.

Case in point: Last week, someone who has been part of the NFL universe for more than 30 years expressed amazement that the Cowboys were favored by only 4.5 points against the Colts. With the Colts in disarray due to multiple trades and poised to make even more trades, the Cowboys would win easily. It was, the person said, a “sure thing.”

There is no sure thing in gambling. Well, there’s one. If you ever let yourself believe you’re smarter than the house and you don’t snap out of it in time, it’s a sure thing that you’ll be filing for bankruptcy, eventually.

Can Dan Quinn and company come up with another way to screw things up in a key moment?

It’s desperation time for New England, like it was when they found a way to beat the Bills.

The Falcons are capable of winning every game. It’s just impossible to know when that version of the team is going to make an appearance.

Time is running out on Houston. And the Browns seem to be in the early stages of a backslide.

One of the craziest weeks in Vikings history ends on a high note, thanks to the Packers not being able to run the ball effectively.

With the uncertainty of the trade deadline over, the Colts can get back to focusing on trying to win football games.

If the Saints can put a full 60 minutes together, they could be very dangerous.

The Bills have a rough six-game stretch coming; they know not to fall victim to a trap game.

Liam Coen and Robert Saleh meet again. One has an elite roster. One does not.

The Cardinals still can’t figure out how to win a game at home.

It’ll take a little time to get Fernando Mendoza fully up to speed.

If it wasn’t already clear that the Cowboys are a long way from being elite, it will be.

Joe Burrow’s MVP case gets even stronger with a prime-time win.

Jacoby Brissett’s job is simple. Win a few winnable games so that the team will still be relevant when Lamar Jackson returns.

#2831. Dan Hicks shared the TV stage in golf and got plenty in return. Expect anything different?

Dan Hicks was at his best when he got everyone on the NBC crew involved in the telecast, and it was one of those moments that led to perhaps his most famous call in golf.

Hicks, who died Wednesday at age 64 after 35 years with NBC, was in the booth for the 2008 U.S. Open. Tiger Woods, playing with shredded ligaments in his left knee and a double stress fracture in his leg, faced a 12-foot birdie putt on the 18th hole at Torrey Pines to force a playoff.

"He asked what it was like on the 18th," Roger Maltbie, who was on the ground for NBC at Torrey Pines, said Thursday. "I said Tiger expected to make it. Everyone expected him to make it. He had that invincible aura at the time."

The putt curled in the right side and Hicks said, "Expect anything different?"

It remains Hicks' most famous call in the 22 times he called the U.S. Open, and for Maltbie, it spoke to what made him so good at his craft and made it easy for everyone around him.

"A very inclusive guy, always upbeat and thoughtful, treated everyone with respect," said Maltbie, who started at NBC the fall before Hicks arrived. "He was a great quarterback, point guard. If you listen to his broadcast, to me he was terrific at passing the ball around. He always had a question, always got you engaged. That's a big part of the job."

Hicks, who also called Notre Dame football and the Olympics, died suddenly of what was described as an acute illness.

He was a warming presence inside and outside the booth, quietly observing players on the range before important rounds, and passing the ball whether it was to Johnny Miller or Paul Azinger, or even when NBC briefly took an unusual route of a four-man booth.

The PGA Tour referred to his partnership with Miller as "one of golf television's defining broadcast partnerships."

Miller said when he retired, "Dan Hicks is so smooth and good it just allows me to do my thing without really a lot of pressure."

"I'm just devastated," Azinger said. "What a void to not have Dan. That's the most impactful man in every aspect of his life. He never got cheated one day out of being happy."

Hicks was remembered as much for how evenly he treated those around him as some of his calls, by which broadcasters often are measured. Hicks certainly had his share.

2012 Ryder CupEurope rallied from a four-point deficit on the final day to stun the Americans in the 2012 Ryder Cup, clinching the victory when Martin Kaymer holed a 6-foot par putt to beat Steve Stricker.

"Europe has pulled off the improbable," Hicks said. "Their own miracle at Medinah."

2025 U.S. Open at OakmontJ.J. Spaun came to the 18th green at Oakmont with a one-shot lead and hit his approach to the left side of the green. He needed two putts from 65 feet to win the 2025 U.S. Open.

"How about one?" Hicks said when Spaun made the memorable putt. "In one of the most unlikely final-round Sundays in U.S. Open history, J.J. Spaun closes the deal with a birdie. ... The stuff you dream about."

2016 Ryder Cup at HazeltineEurope had won three straight Ryder Cups when the American team finally won at Hazeltine. The winning point came from a captain's pick, Ryan Moore, who two-putted for par from 10 feet.

"The European streak is finally over. Ryan Moore has provided the difference, and the United States will begin a celebration that will ring into the night and beyond," Hicks said.

2024 U.S. Open at Pinehurst No. 2Hicks was on the ground for NBC in 1999 at Pinehurst No. 2 when Payne Stewart famously hit wedge to 15 feet and saved par to win the U.S. Open. He was in the booth when Bryson DeChambeau got up-and-down with a 55-yard bunker shot and 4-foot putt to win by one.

"Another epic up-and-down at Pinehurst for U.S. Open championship," Hicks said. "Bryson DeChambeau now has two, the second at No. 2."

Memories of the voice and the manJimmy Roberts recalls leaning on Hicks the first time he had to do play-by-play, and how Hicks guided him along as someone who loved to see others succeed.

"I think there are so many giants in our industry who are out there doing such incredible work, but Dan never made it about himself," Roberts said. "I don't know that he ever got the credit that he deserves."

Hicks was the voice during the Florida swing on the PGA Tour, including The Players Championship. He was there for Nelly Korda winning the U.S. Women's Open at Riviera, the return of Woods at the 2018 Tour Championship and so many Ryder Cups.

"The beauty of his job is that we'll all remember that voice and the stories he told and the kind of experiences he gave us through calling golf," Max Homa said Thursday at the Austin Championship. "I'm glad I've got my memories with him because they were always great."

#2830. Warriors reveal Stephen Curry injury diagnosis after MRI on abdomen

The Golden State Warriors were already without one star. Now they'll be without their Most Valuable Player candidate, Stephen Curry, for the short-term future, too.

The Warriors announced Thursday, Nov. 12 that an MRI confirmed Curry suffered an oblique abdominal tear and added that he would be reevaluated in two weeks. The injury took place Wednesday, Nov. 11 in an eventual 115-108 loss against the Houston Rockets.

Curry walked off the floor midway through the first quarter, clearly grimacing and favoring his right side.

The sequence took place just 5:35 into the game. Curry had scored 3 points on 1-of-3 shooting by that point. He did not return to the floor for the rest of the first quarter, and he was not back at the bench area to start the second period.

ANALYSIS: What does Curry injury mean for Warriors? Can they weather the storm?

The Warriors announced minutes into the second quarter that Curry would not return for the rest of the game with right abdominal soreness.

After the game, Warriors coach Steve Kerr said that Curry said he "tweaked something in his abdominal area," which Curry told Kerr he had never done before. Kerr added that "concern is high" though he reiterated that he had no idea of the extent of the injury.

During the game, Curry did have a collision with Rockets forward Amen Thompson earlier in the game, when Curry had driven to the hoop on an attempted layup. Then, Curry had been fighting off a screen against Rockets center Alperen Şengün. As soon as that sequence ended, Curry once again grabbed his side.

Finally, just moments before he left the game, Curry curled around a screen and put up a 3-point shot. As soon as he landed and stepped backwards, he grabbed at his right side, near his hip.

Curry had been in the midst of a renaissance and had been elevating the play of the Warriors, who entered Wednesday night 8-3, which was tied for the third best record in the Western Conference.

Prior to suffering the injury, Curry was averaging 30.6 points, 4.1 rebounds and 4.3 assists per game in nine appearances. He was converting 3-pointers at a blistering 45.7%, which would mark a career-high. His scoring total entering Wednesday night had ranked second in the NBA, behind only Luka Dončić's historic 39.3 points per game, for all players with at least five games played.

The Warriors were already without Jimmy Butler, who has missed the start of the season as he rehabs the torn anterior cruciate ligament he suffered in January 2026.

#2828. Lions vs. Patriots matches up this year's two most aggressive head coaches on fourth downs

When the Lions meet the Patriots in Munich on Sunday, the fans in Germany are likely to see both teams go for it on fourth down.

That’s because Lions head coach Dan Campbell has long been the league’s most aggressive coach on fourth downs, and this year, Patriots coach Mike Vrabel is No. 2.

Coaches’ fourth down decisions are tracked for the Aggressiveness Index at FTN Fantasy, looking at how often coaches go for it compared to other coaches in the same situation. By that measurement, Campbell is first with a score of 1.45 (meaning he goes for it 45 percent more than an average NFL coach) and Vrabel is second with a score of 1.22.

The Aggressiveness Index is a more detailed way of tracking a coach’s fourth-down decision making than simply looking at the number of times a team has gone for it on fourth down. Some teams go for it often on fourth down not because the coach is aggressive, but because they happen to face a lot of fourth-and-shorts while trailing late in the fourth quarter. That’s not a particularly aggressive time to go for it.

What gives Campbell such a high score on the Aggressiveness Index is decisions like going for it on fourth down at his own 24-yard line with the score tied late in the fourth quarter against the Packers. That’s the kind of call that Campbell makes and other coaches don’t, which is why his Aggressiveness Index is so much higher than any other coach’s.

Campbell is at or near the top of the Aggressiveness Index every year. Last year he was second only to Mike Kafka, who was very aggressive on fourth downs during his seven-game stint as the Giants’ interim head coach. Perhaps unsurprisingly, after Kafka was not retained by the Giants, Campbell hired him to be the Lions’ passing game coordinator.

Vrabel has not been as aggressive as Campbell on fourth downs, but he has been more aggressive than the average NFL coach, both during his time with the Patriots and before that as head coach of the Titans. Both Vrabel and Campbell are former players with reputations for a blend of old-school toughness and a new-school understanding of the strategic advantages of aggressive fourth-down decision making. It’s a winning combination.

#2827. Robinhood shares fall as crypto slump weighs on profits, offsetting gains in new businesses

Robinhood shares were down around 8% in pre-market trading on Wednesday as investors digested weaker-than-expected third-quarter earnings despite growth in options trading and prediction markets.

Here's how the brokerage performed in its quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with Wall Street estimates, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

Robinhood's net income fell to $468 million, or 51 cents per share, from $556 million, or 61 cents per share, a year ago. Revenue represented a 12% increase from $1.27 billion in the same quarter last year.

Despite that gain, transaction-based revenue of $867 million came in below Wall Street estimates from StreetAccount of $879 million, putting focus on whether Robinhood can sustain momentum across its trading businesses and reduce its reliance on volatile market activity.

Robinhood has been diversifying its business as it tries to expand its brokerage app into a broader financial services platform. Options trading and event contracts have become increasingly important sources of transaction revenue, while the company has continued to invest in wealth management, subscriptions and other offerings.

Transaction-based revenue grew 19% from the same period last year to $867 million, driven primarily by a more than 500% increase in event contracts revenue. Additionally, options revenue grew 31% to $399 million and equities revenue rose 41% to $121 million.

Those gains were partially offset by cryptocurrency revenue, which fell 55% to $120 million.

Chief Financial Officer Shiv Verma told CNBC event contracts on crypto movements did well in the quarter, offering investors another way to express a view on crypto despite the slump in prices and spot trading.

Net deposits on the Robinhood platform totaled $15.7 billion for the quarter, equal to a 17% annualized growth rate based on total platform assets at the end of Q2 2026. Over the past year, net deposits totaled $71 billion, representing 21% growth compared with total platform assets at the end of Q3 2025.

—With reporting from CNBC's Robert Hum and Kate Rooney

#2826. Zidane: ‘Italy played better in France, but they won tonight’

Zinedine Zidane feels Italy 'played better in Paris, but they won this evening' when inflicting his first defeat as France coach, and defends Chelsea's Malo Gusto from criticism.

Les Bleus had got off to the ideal start at San Siro when Michael Olise pounced on a Gianluigi Donnarumma howler playing out from the back.

Francesco Pio Esposito equalised, but they felt that when Sebastiano Esposito blazed a penalty wide, it would be a turning point in the match.

Instead, the Azzurri got back on their feet quickly, winning 3-1 with Davide Frattesi and another penalty, this time converted by Pio Esposito.

MILAN – NOVEMBER 12: Zinedine Zidane, Head Coach of France, looks on before the UEFA Nations League 2026/27 group phase match between Italy and France at San Siro Stadium on November 12, 2026 in Milan. (Photo by Mattia Ozbot/Getty Images)

"We had a good initial approach and held the ball well, but after the break we lost our way, especially defensively," confessed Zidane in his press conference.

This was an unusual situation, as before tonight France had not scored in the first half in any of their last nine matches.

"We stayed in the game, did good things in possession, but we fell away in the second half, especially after going 2-1 down. It is part of the process."

France were decimated by injuries, missing Inter's Marcus Thuram, Juventus defender Pierre Kalulu, Roma midfielder Manu Koné, Kylian Mbappé, Ousmane Dembélé, Aurelien Tchouameni, William Saliba, Jules Koundé, Maxence Lacroix, Maghnes Akliouche and Esteban Lepaul.

Malo Gusto had terrible problems at right-back dealing with Nicolò Cambiaghi first, then Federico Dimarco.

MILAN – NOVEMBER 12: Nicolo Cambiaghi of Italy is challenged by Malo Gusto and Dayot Upamecano of France during the UEFA Nations League 2026/27 group phase match between Italy and France at San Siro Stadium on November 12, 2026 in Milan. (Photo by Mattia Ozbot/Getty Images)

"Your job is to analyse what you need to analyse, but my focus is on recharging the batteries for the next game," replied Zidane to questions about the Chelsea defender.

"We are all united, we didn't defend well in general as a team and need to improve. I won't change players over one game. Malo Gusto gave away a penalty, but it can happen."

Olise scored the opener, but then made little impact as the match wore on at San Siro.

"You cannot always play at the top level for 90 minutes. Olise had some good performances, he wasn't the only one who fell away in the second half, the whole team did.

"We wanted to win the game tonight to ensure we had first place on Sunday, now we must win the final match against Turkiye, but that was the objective anyway regardless of this evening's result."

MILAN – NOVEMBER 12: Zinedine Zidane, Head Coach of France, looks on before the UEFA Nations League 2026/27 group phase match between Italy and France at San Siro Stadium on November 12, 2026 in Milan. (Photo by Mattia Ozbot/Getty Images)

Mbappé was not even on the bench this evening, but could he recover in time for the final group game with Turkiye on Sunday?

"We hope so, that is the plan and why he has not gone back home. We just decided not to risk it tonight over a slight problem."

#2824. Emotional Harry Kane reflects on ‘crazy few weeks’ after setting another England record

Harry Kane reflected on a "crazy few weeks" after scoring the winning goal against Croatia on his record-breaking 126th appearance for England.

The England captain struck a record-extending 92nd international goal to seal a 2-1 win over Croatia at Wembley on Thursday night, a result that confirmed the team's progress into the quarter-finals of the Nations League.

The Ballon d'Or winner became England's most capped men's player of all-time in that match, having equalled the record held by Peter Shilton last month. Shilton was on hand to make a pre-match presentation to the star striker, along with the Prince of Wales.

England had beaten Slaven Bilic's side 7-0 in Croatia 40 days earlier, but this was a much more keenly contested affair. Kane was happy England got the job done, setting up a top-of-the-table shootout against Spain on Sunday.

"It's been a crazy few weeks," Kane told BBC Radio 5 Live. "Of course it takes a lot out of you emotionally, physically and mentally and all these things.

Kane was honoured with a tifo before kick-off (PA)

"I'm very good at staying focused on the job and what needs to be done but it's a hard balance because you want to take it in. Breaking the record is a massive thing in the history of our game, seeing the tifo there, having my kids with me, and being presented by all the people was beyond dreams.

"Once the whistle goes and you're on the pitch, you just start focusing on what your job is, pressing and playing the ball, playing assists and goals and that just takes over. It's always been in me that I'm able to switch, and of course now I can enjoy it with my family on the pitch after this.

"I've always said all these big milestones that I've had, when you win the game it makes it 10 times better and I've been lucky enough to win all of them, especially here at Wembley so it's nice to do it again."

Spain defeated Czechia 2-0 in Prague on Thursday as the world champions extended their historic unbeaten streak to 43 matches. That run stretches back to March 2024.

Luis de la Fuente's side had to come from a goal behind to beat England 3-2 in the reverse fixture in September. Kane is looking forward to the return contest at Madrid's Metropolitano Stadium.

"After the first game when we lost here against them, we knew we left a bit out there, we knew we could have done a little bit better," Kane added on ITV.

"We said to ourselves 'let's win every game from now until the next one and see what happens'. It's the perfect opportunity now to try and prove ourselves."

#2821. 49ers pivoted to Keenan Allen trade after initially focusing on Josh Downs

The 49ers made a trade for wide receiver Keenan Allen on Tuesday, but he wasn’t the only Colts wide receiver on their radar ahead of the trade deadline.

During a Thursday appearance on KNBR, General Manager John Lynch said that the team turned its attention to Allen after initially targeting Josh Downs as an addition to their receiver room. Lynch said that head coach Kyle Shanahan brought up Allen while watching film on Downs and conversations with the Colts began before they landed defensive tackle DeForest Buckner in an earlier deal with Indianapolis.

“We kind of focused on Josh Downs as an option that we’d have some interest,” Lynch said, via 49ersWebzone.com. “And Kyle and I are kind of watching Josh, and in the process, Kyle’s like, ‘Man, what about Keenan?’ And I was like, ‘You know, we hadn’t given much thought to that, but let me ask about that.’ And so, there started a conversation about two and a half weeks ago, really, when the DeForest talks [happened]. ‘Would he?’ And it’s always fluid, and at a certain time, they didn’t want to give up Keenan. It kind of wasn’t going to be on the table, but we kept communicating, and ultimately we got him.”

The 49ers sent a 2027 sixth-round pick to the Colts for Allen. Downs and a 2027 seventh-round pick were eventually traded to the Chiefs for 2027 fourth- and fifth-round picks.

#2820. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long haul

The ongoing earnings season, Middle East tensions and buzz ahead of the U.S. midterm elections have been keeping investors busy. Further, worries about elevated artificial intelligence spending are weighing on investor sentiment.

Investors looking beyond the short-term noise and focusing on long-term returns can track top Wall Street analysts to pick the right stocks, backed by strong research.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first stock pick is Datadog, an AI-powered observability and security platform. The company will announce its results for the third quarter on Nov. 4. Datadog is seeing strong demand for its offerings, thanks to cloud migration and rapid AI adoption. TipRanks' KPIs tool for DDOG shows the growth in its customer base over the past few years amid increasing market penetration and growing adoption of its products.

Heading into Q3 FY26 earnings, Bank of America analyst Koji Ikeda reiterated a buy rating on Datadog stock and raised his price target to $335 from $305. The analyst noted that industry checks and data indicate that infrastructure software demand is strengthening, with AI increasingly driving usage and growth.

Ikeda called Datadog and Atlassian his top picks, saying that companies that can show that AI is a tailwind to growth could see their valuation rerate, even at premium multiples. He added that the total addressable market for observability is growing faster than anticipated, driven by accelerated growth of enterprise AI and the rapid ramp of consumer AI agents.

The five-star analyst believes that focus will be on revenue growth, which he estimates to be 28.7% for Q3 FY26 versus the Street's consensus of 29.1%. Ikeda thinks that the market is underestimating the growth prospects of DDOG's security products. Meanwhile, he cautioned investors about concentration risk related to large customers.

"We think results will be supportive of our long-term thesis that Datadog is a strong share gainer in the rapidly expanding observability opportunity," said Ikeda.

Ikeda ranks No. 673 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 55% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.9%. See Datadog Statistics on TipRanks.

The next pick, semiconductor giant Nvidia, is also benefiting from AI-driven demand for its products. After attending Nvidia's GTC Berlin conference, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reaffirmed a buy rating on NVDA stock with a price target of $300, calling it a top pick in the semiconductor space.

The five-star analyst said that the conference supported his thesis that Nvidia continues to effectively navigate bottlenecks in the supply chain, land/power/shell, and funding. In fact, Moore is "convinced that all 3 aspects are opportunities for NVDA."

While Nvidia's GTC Berlin offered no major updates, Moore noted the conference reflected that the company continues to add to its significant advantages heading into 2027. The analyst said that Vera Rubin is gaining momentum, though details on next year's Rubin Ultra were lighter than expected.

With demand outpacing supply, Moore said that focus is on closing the gap. In this regard, he highlighted the supply chain deals on substrate, packaging and testing. While the analyst continues to view TSMC 3 nm wafer supply as the biggest challenge, he expects it to ease over time.

Moore ranks No. 142 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.4%. See Nvidia Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Aerospace company SpaceX recently announced solid third-quarter results, with its AI revenue surging to $7.56 billion from $813 million in the prior-year quarter.

Impressed by the results, Needham analyst Ryan Koontz reiterated a buy rating on SPCX stock with a price target of $250. Koontz highlighted that SpaceX's Q3 results significantly exceeded expectations, proving that the company's vertically integrated business model is driving massive scale.

Furthermore, Koontz emphasized the 195.2% sequential growth in SpaceX's AI revenue, fueled by wholesale spot compute deals. He noted management's increasing focus on its AI strategy, given that it is optimistic about adding 10 gigawatts of incremental capacity during 2027.

"With increasing confidence on Starship re-use, we meaningfully increase our estimates," said Koontz.

Koontz ranks No. 357 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 42% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.7%. See SpaceX Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2819. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 dividend stocks for consistent income

Democrats' midterm election win has put the artificial intelligence trade in question, as voters clearly expressed opposition to the rapid buildout of data centers. The stock market could remain volatile amid AI trade concerns, macro uncertainties, and geopolitical tensions.

In this scenario, investors can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios to earn steady income. However, the extensive universe of dividend stocks can make selection challenging. Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as these experts assign ratings after in-depth financial analysis.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Viper Energy (VNOM) is a subsidiary of Diamondback and owns and acquires mineral and royalty interests, primarily in the Permian Basin. The company reported upbeat third-quarter earnings, supported by recent acquisitions and continued development activity from Diamondback and third-party operators. Viper announced a base cash dividend of 50 cents per share for Q3 2026, payable on Nov. 19. Based on the base and variable dividends announced over the past 12 months, VNOM's dividend yield stands at 5.7%.

Following the Q3 results, TD Cowen analyst Aaron Bilkoski reiterated a buy rating on Viper Energy stock with a price target of $61. The analyst noted that the company delivered solid Q3 results, with production, including oil and natural gas liquids, exceeding both TD Cowen and consensus estimates.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that the key update was the announcement of two royalty acquisitions for $809 million in cash, including deferred consideration, and $82 million of newly issued shares.

Given the recently announced base dividend hike, Bilkoski expects Viper to direct incremental free cash flow toward buybacks. While the acquisitions increase leverage, the analyst believes that Viper has enough capacity for opportunistic buybacks.

Bilkoski noted that Viper has historically delivered one of the highest production per share growth profiles in his royalty coverage, but still trades at a notable discount to the royalty peer average and the company's historical FCF yield range. "In our view, its above-average growth warrants a higher valuation," said the analyst.

Bilkoski ranks No. 525 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.80%. See Viper Energy Stock Buybacks on TipRanks.

Exxon Mobil (XOM) reported robust Q3 2026 profits, as its U.S. refineries boosted production to capitalize on the high diesel fuel prices sparked by the conflict in the Middle East. The oil and gas giant announced a quarterly dividend of $1.07 per share, payable on Dec. 10, reflecting a 3.9% increase. This marks Exxon's 44th consecutive year of dividend increases. At an annualized dividend of $4.28 per share, XOM offers a yield of 2.53%.

Impressed by the Q3 print, TD Cowen analyst Jason Gabelman reiterated a buy rating on Exxon stock and raised his price target to $193 from $180 to reflect higher downstream earnings, with the expectation of increased mid-cycle margins.

Gabelman noted that the strength in Exxon's refining business continues to offset Middle East-related headwinds. He added that the company increased its ownership in LNG projects, which should reduce its Middle East concentration over time from 75% to 50% and lower the likelihood of diversification-focused mergers and acquisitions. Beyond LNG, Gabelman expects Exxon's growth in the 2030s to come from Guyana, Kashagan, Permian, and possibly Venezuela.

Gabelman noted that Exxon announced a dividend hike of nearly 4% and plans to maintain buybacks at $20 billion for 2027. "The decision to maintain reduces the pro-cyclicality nature of the buyback," said the analyst.

Gabelman ranks No. 347 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.90%. See Exxon Mobil Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Williams (WMB), which owns and operates energy infrastructure to deliver natural gas, reported strong third-quarter results. The company announced a quarterly dividend of $0.525 per share, or $2.10 per share annualized, payable on Dec. 28. WMB offers a dividend yield of 3.09%.

In reaction to the Q3 2026 results, CIBC analyst Robert Catellier reaffirmed a buy rating on Williams stock with a price target of $82. The analyst noted that Q3 results were essentially in line with expectations and reflected continued strength in execution across WMB's infrastructure portfolio.

Catellier said that Williams' Q3 performance was driven by higher transmission, storage, and power generation revenues, as well as contributions from the Momentum Midstream acquisition.

He noted that Williams maintained its full-year EBITDA guidance of $8.3 billion to $8.5 billion. In fact, results are tracking toward the higher end of the guidance range, backed by contributions from recently completed projects and the Momentum Midstream deal. WMB continues to target long-term EBITDA compound annual growth rate of at least 11% through 2030.

Catellier expects WMB's long-term targets to be supported by incremental transmission and storage opportunities, including Transco's Southeast Supply Enhancement II project and an 84 MMcf/d expansion of the Garden Connector project.

Catellier ranks No. 667 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 9.60%. See Williams Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2817. Minnesota Timberwolves: First Quarter Conferences

As a former teacher, conferences were always a bit of a mixed bag. On the hard side were the additional four hours of work tacked on top of a school day and the social anxiety of having the first in-depth conversations with parents, never fully knowing what kind of energy they would bring to the conversation. However, whether a student was struggling or thriving, the majority of the time, the conversations were very beneficial in getting everyone on the same page.

Each conversation really came down to the three main points:

The question of "Is there anything I can be doing as a teacher to better support them?"

In our season-long grading project, this is the first opportunity to track progress and get a look at how the categories, statistical ranges, and grade direction are shaping up. These stats reflect the first 11 games of the Minnesota Timberwolves' season, so the sample size is small, but large enough to begin to get a sense of the good, the bad, and to see if there are better ways to capture what each player is doing on the floor in their role.

1. These grades are roles-based, so the stats I'm looking at for each player are different and meant to highlight their team role.

2. Roles on the team can change as the roster and playing time changes—I will alter or add statistical categories throughout the season as needed. Especially in a preseason article, the expectation is that some of these initial stats will change as we get to know the team better.

3. The grade ranges and their correlation to stats are educated speculation based on previous career averages, team role, and minutes expectation. Like the categories themselves, if there is a range that is clearly missing the eye test, expect some tweaks as the season goes along.

First off, the fact that you are well on your way to a perfect attendance award is remarkable. While the season is long and nobody is going to count any chickens before they hatch, going 11/11 in games played deserves an immediate gold star. Well done!

You know what else deserves a gold star? The team offense. Despite losing the team's most important offensive cog, you've helped hold things together and guide the squad to the seventh-best offensive rating in the league, a number that, wherever it ends up after thirty-ish games, will probably be the floor of the season.

Now let's talk about rebounding. Honestly, it turns out this may just be a bad stat to track for you on this team. The team is rebounding fantastically to start the season as other players have stepped up. I mean, if you are an English major, who cares about your math scores, right? So maybe we need to focus on something else going forward…

Ah, yes. There it is. Four turnovers a game. 5.2 turnovers per 36 minutes, which would be the second-worst in the NBA for rotation players. That number is getting alarmingly close to being one that could soft-launch a bakery. Let's clean that up a bit and bring it closer to your career average of 3.2 going forward, okay?

*Sigh* There isn't much that can be said with just a four-game sample size of numbers, so I'll just use this space to opine for a moment.

This Wolves team is the most excited I've been about a group since the 23-24 squad had their meteoric rise and won over our hearts and minds with defensive grit and intensity. This season's squad is flashing that kind of defensive performance too… but only with an offensive ceiling to mirror it, assuming a healthy Anthony Edwards.

Teams that have both a top-five offense and defense often play a lot of basketball deep into the month of May and beyond. Get healthy soon, Ant!

Don't tell the other students, but you continue to be my favorite. Efficient shooting from deep and midrange, and being a key component of the number one defense in the league? Night in and night out, this team knows exactly what it is going to get out of you and, quite frankly, it's phenomenal.

Sometimes you have a conference that is short and sweet because nothing about the student's effort and production needs to be changed. Keep doing what you are doing, and we'll do everything we can to get you your first All-Star trip!

Welcome to Minnesota, Mr. Kuminga! Much like Jaden McDaniels, your work on the defensive end has been astounding. It is a wholly un-Wolves-like experience to go into matchups with the variety of power forwards there are in the league and feel as though you have a matchup for them. Raising your defensive EPM to an 86th percentile defender is JUST what the doctor ordered for this roster.

Here's the curious thing – you are shooting 40% from deep and yet have a near career low in true shooting percentage? Let me check the numbers here… ah okay. There it is. 52.5% from the free throw line. Have you been spending too much time with Mr. Gobert outside of class? As a career 70% shooter from the line, let's clean that up a bit and you'll see your grade in the "A" range in no time!

Let's talk about the good first – you are the anchor of the number one defense in the league through 11 games. For everything else that we are going to talk about, that's the most important thing, and it's the reason that you are on this team. You are one of the greatest defenders to ever play the game, and once again, anchoring a top defense in the league is truly a special career achievement.

It is really important that you understand how much we all appreciate your defense before anything else is said, okay?

But my God, has the goatee grown so thick that it is affecting your peripheral vision and ability to hit a layup? A non-rookie season career-low of 50% from the floor? Just 1.7 dunks per game despite having one of the best passing point guards in the league? This was supposed to be an apex offensive Gobert season!

I should note that your turnover numbers are at a non-rookie season career low… which is something… but that something may be YOUR INABILITY TO CATCH A PASS, INCREASING EVERYONE'S TURNOVERS AROUND YOU!! If the league tracked fumbled passes, you'd be having A WILT CHAMBERLAIN-ESQUE RUN!!!

Okay, I just had to get that out of my system. It had been brewing for a while, and conferences are a time for honest feedback, right? Plus, my therapist told me I should let it out so I'd stop mumbling "catch the damn ball, Rudy" in my sleep. Please re-read the first paragraph to know what is most important, and then let's just be better on the offensive end, okay?

An "A-" grade that mostly reflects the slow-cooking EPM metrics. If you were to keep consistently playing the kind of basketball you are, that EPM will be in the green in no time!

That points-per-shot-attempt number continues to be elite, and if this NBA season plays out similarly to previous ones, it would be a 95th percentile number, which is remarkable considering the increased offensive load and defensive focus. That PIE number (player impact estimate) would put you inside the top forty players in the league – quite the value for a player making $19 million this season.

Since the Anthony Edwards injury, no one has upped their usage more and with that, you've been the team's leading scorer over the past seven games, averaging 21.6 points per game. Turns out the "As You Wish" bench superstar is just a fantastic basketball player.

A B+ grade in your role to start the year because you are doing EXACTLY what is needed. Incremental steps of improvement to earn minutes, flashing an elite skill (offensive rebounding), and utilizing hustle to minimize mistakes and earn the coaching staff's trust.

Would I like to see a few more blocks per game? Sure, but it is clear that part of your game is being intentionally restrained to help you stay out of foul trouble as you keep getting your feet wet. Like a good stew, we're just going to keep letting you cook in a limited role that will pay dividends later in the season.

Before we begin, I need to ask you a question. Have you been turning in your "finishing at the rim" homework? Because the numbers here tell me that we're actually on pace to finish worse than last season's 52.9%, which was an area of improvement we had marked over the summer.

You're thriving within the concept of team defense (a huge jump from the preseason's -1.1 mark!), and you are getting to the rim and drawing fouls at more than twice the level of any time in your career. Talk about excelling in your role!

The only thing that is missing is a bit more of that deft touch around the basket to complement your elite downhill first step. Far be it from me to try to activate a healthy inter-team competition, but here's where the rest of the roster stands when finishing within 5ft of the basket:

Maybe everyone on the team should be spending more time with Trey Lyles. Speaking of…

I'll be honest with you, Mr. Lyles – when you transferred here, the only real sense of your skill set I had was a player who could space the floor with shooting and not much else. It is a testament to how wrong I am that the shooting really hasn't come around yet, and you are being a player who is consistently driving winning.

The defensive rating and defensive EPM (1.1) are really encouraging. In fact, that EPM number is fourth on the team, only behind Gobert, McDaniels, and Kuminga!

Know what else has been surprising? Your ability as a rebounder and, dare I even say it, a dunker?! Like most Timberwolves fans, these first 11 games have invoked a lot of "I was not familiar with your game" utterances. Start hitting those threes, and you could be hunting the ever-elusive A+ grade.

Let's talk about the scoring. That 25.1 points per thirty-six minutes is astounding. It would put you within the top twenty-five of the league on a list that includes mostly current NBA All-Stars. That you are doing it from more than just beyond the arc is the other really encouraging thing! This Wolves team needs a flamethrower scorer while Anthony Edwards is out, and you've stepped up to be just that.

I cannot explain the pace number being so low, but I am keeping it for now as a stand-in for the rest of your game. Despite the scoring inferno, you are the only rotation player to have a negative net rating (-10.1), a 15-point swing from the next closest rotation player. Because of this, your EPM numbers have dropped more than anyone else on the team, which means there is probably quite a bit lacking on the defensive end. An elite offensive game with a bad defensive game will land you comfortably in that "C" range.

I really don't want to jinx this, so I'm just going to whisper it between us, okay?

… Did you know that you are shooting 38% from deep this season on over two attempts per game? If this were to be maintained, when partnered with your defensive splash/hustle plays, it may turn out that there is a real and consistent role in the rotation for you. A player who has a limited role, steps into it, and goes beyond to earn more minutes? That is a role-based "A" if I've ever seen one!

A video of Jaylen Clark doing a Jaylen Clark thing is a perfect way to close out these first conferences because it encapsulates so much of what we've loved about this team so far. I'll be back in ten games with the finalized first-quarter report cards!

#2815. Tokenization platform Securitize gains 10% postmarket on Q3 results, move into asset-backed finance

Tokenization platform Securitize climbed about 10% postmarket after reporting third quarter growth alongside increased operating expenses, and announcing an expansion into asset-backed finance through a strategic acquisition.

Miami-based Securitize, which made its public market debut in July, operates in the relatively new corner of the crypto equity universe involved in institutional tokenization infrastructure. Tokenized assets on the platform grew 9% in the third quarter, to $4.8 billion, from the same period a year ago. Aggregate transaction volume rose 20% from a year ago, to $4.3 billion. Revenue of $11.5 million was up 28% from last year.

The growth came as bullish sentiment around tokenization gained traction throughout the third quarter, ahead of the Security and Exchange Commission formalizing the long awaited Innovation Exemption on Sept. 17, which created a temporary pathway for trading of certain tokenized stocks and strengthened the regulatory outlook.

Profitability is still meaningfully below year-ago levels, due in part to costs associated with going public over the summer, as well as the scaling up of the tokenization business at an active time for the entire sector, Securitize CEO Carlos Domingo told CNBC in an exclusive interview.

Securitize reinforced "certain areas of the company like finance, compliance, legal [and] cybersecurity," which increased corporate costs "compared to a year ago when we were privately held," Domingo said. "We're in in a very good position. The adjusted EBITDA … is improving significantly from previous quarters."

Securitize reported an adjusted EBITDA loss of $2 million, compared with positive adjusted EBITDA of $3.6 million a year ago, better than a $5.5 million loss in the second quarter. The company posted a 183% increase in operating costs and expenses.

Also on Monday, the Securitize board authorized a $50 million share buyback and announced the acquisition of River One, an institutional asset-backed financing platform developing technology to connect stablecoin capital with traditional lending markets. Terms were not disclosed.

Securitize sees an opportunity to modernize the $9 trillion market for asset backed finance by using stablecoins to finance real world assets, generate yield and tokenize investment positions and credit products, Domingo said.The acquisition also marks an expansion beyond Securitize's core niche markets at a time when tokenization's competitive landscape is growing, with traditional asset managers like BlackRock and Franklin Templeton, and established financial market infrastructure providers like DTCC and Broadridge, entering the industry.

"We have been very active in tokenized funds, treasuries [and] yield … And we've been obviously recently very active in tokenized stocks, so this adds another piece of the puzzle in the emerging tokenization space for us to capture revenue growth."

#2814. Jared Verse upset with how he's playing, met with team psychiatrist about "mental block"

Browns defensive end Jared Verse still doesn’t have a sack this season, and he says his struggles are more mental than physical.

Verse told reporters in Cleveland today that he has been frustrated and needed to work on himself to get right.

“I’m upset with the way I’ve been playing. I don’t think it’s anything physical, nothing about my body, it’s the mental aspect,” Verse said.

Verse revealed that he has worked with Mayur Pandya, the Browns’ on-site mental health professional, to work through his struggles.

“I met with Dr. P a little bit to figure out what my issue was,” Verse said. “Our team psychiatrist.”

Verse said he wants to be a leader of the defensive line and knows he can’t be that if he’s not taking personal inventory of how he can improve.

“I’m mainly going to put it on myself: I haven’t played well at all this season,” Verse said. “I think I’ve taken a little bit of time looking in the mirror at what I could do better because I think the group goes as I go. That’s my mindset in anything I do.”

Verse said he’s confident that he’s conquering the mental issues holding him back.

“I think it was definitely a mental block,” Verse said. “I train my body every day to push myself to the limits of what I’m physically capable of doing. So I don’t think it was any issue with my body, but I think I’ve taken the steps to figure out what it was.”
